# Complete item spec
### P1.6 — Атомарная квота и счётчик ревизий

**Intent:** Квота storage не превышается при параллельных загрузках и копиях, номера ревизий файла идут без дублей и
дыр, дельта квоты считается от фактически перечитанной головы, а все операции берут блокировки в каноническом порядке D21
(drive → узлы по id → агрегат → квота → CAS) с ретраем транзакции при deadlock/serialization (E2, AD21, I7; V02, V03, V20, V21).

**Why:** OPUS 13 P1.6, 02 D21, 08 §1 (I7), §5. Сегодня квота проверяется до транзакции по модели в памяти
(`packages/drive/src/Actions/UploadFileAction.php:78, 245–261`), а `adjustUsedBytes` (`packages/core/src/Repositories/Drive/StorageStoreRepository.php:59–74`)
прибавляет без условия; номер ревизии — `max(version_no) + 1` без блокировки (`packages/core/src/Repositories/Drive/FileVersionStoreRepository.php:40–49`);
`previousHeadSize` читается до транзакции; `version_count` увеличивается от значения в памяти (`FileStoreRepository.php:46`).

**Scope Included:**
1. `StorageStoreRepository::reserveBytes(Storage $storage, int $delta): void` (new) — при `$delta > 0` внутри транзакции
   вызывающего: `UPDATE <storages> SET used_bytes = used_bytes + ? WHERE id = ? AND (quota_bytes IS NULL OR used_bytes + ? <= quota_bytes)`
   на `VaulterDatabase::connection()`; 0 строк → перечитать `quota_bytes`/`used_bytes` и бросить
   `QuotaExceededException::forStorage(...)` (транзакция откатывается); при `$delta <= 0` — существующий `adjustUsedBytes`
   (освобождение с ограничением снизу 0). Предпроверка `assertWithinQuota()` остаётся только как быстрый отказ.
2. CAS-коммит готовых байтов (уровень 5 после уровня 4): `Contracts\Cas\BlobStore::commitStaged(StagedObject $staged, BlobScope $scope, ?string $disk = null): Blob` (new);
   `media BlobStore::commit()` = `stage()` + `commitStaged()` + `discard()` в `finally`; `commitStaged` не вызывает `discard()`.
3. `UploadFileAction::executeUpload()` — порядок:
   1) до транзакции: `$staged = ContentStore::stage($data->stream)` (размер и sha известны), `try { … } finally { $staged->discard(); }`;
   2) `VaulterDatabase::transaction()`: `lockForUpdate([parent])` (уровень 2, P1.2; для новой ревизии — `[node]`) → для новой
      ревизии `FileStoreRepository::lockForUpdate(File)` (new, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` строки `files`, уровень 3) и перечитанная
      голова (`current_version_id` → её `size_bytes`; нет головы → 0) → `delta = $staged->sizeBytes() - $headSize` →
      `reserveBytes` при `delta > 0`, иначе `adjustUsedBytes(delta)` при `delta < 0` (уровень 4) →
      `commitStaged($staged, BlobScope::forUpload(...), $disk)` (уровень 5) → строки node/file/version/edge → голова;
   3) номер ревизии = `$lockedFile->version_count + 1` (новый файл — 1); `FileStoreRepository::repointHead` пишет
      `version_count` = этот номер (не `+1` от модели в памяти); `FileVersionStoreRepository::nextVersionNo()` удаляется (документы
      используют свой `NodeDocumentVersionStoreRepository::nextVersionNo` — без изменений, R8).
4. `CopyNodeAction`: `adjustUsedBytes($addedBytes)` → `reserveBytes($addedBytes)` в той же транзакции (одна операция на сумму
   поддерева; превышение откатывает копию целиком).
5. Порядок блокировок: в `UploadFileAction`, `CopyNodeAction`, `MoveNodeAction`, `RestoreNodeAction`, `ForceDeleteNodeAction`
   уровень блокировки не убывает (D21); над последовательностью в `UploadFileAction` — однострочный комментарий
   `// Lock order: drive → nodes → file → quota → CAS, so concurrent writers never deadlock.`
6. Ретраи: `VaulterDatabase::transaction($callback, 3)` (P1.1, R4) — замыкания `UploadFileAction`/`CopyNodeAction` не имеют
   побочных эффектов вне БД до коммита, кроме promote объекта (сага: при откате — сирота для reconcile).
7. Тесты (PG, `ForkRace`, `DatabaseMigrations`, каталог `packages/drive/tests/Concurrency/`):
   - V02 — `QuotaRaceTest.php`: квота 10 МБ, свободно 10 МБ; две задачи загружают разные файлы по 8 МБ на разных соединениях →
     ровно одна успешна, другая `failedWith(QuotaExceededException::class)`; `used_bytes` = сумма `size_bytes` голов всех файлов
     storage; строки blob проигравшей нет; `vaulter:reconcile-blobs` после grace не удаляет байты победителя.
     Негативный контроль: временно заменить `reserveBytes` на безусловный `adjustUsedBytes` → обе успешны (`used_bytes` 16 МБ) — красный.
   - V03 — `RevisionNumberRaceTest.php`: файл с ревизией 1; две параллельные новые ревизии → номера `{2, 3}` без дублей и дыр;
     `files.version_count = 3`; голова — ревизия с номером 3; `used_bytes` = размер головы (дельты от реальных предыдущих голов).
     Негативный контроль: убрать `FileStoreRepository::lockForUpdate` → дубль номера/нарушение уникальности хотя бы в одном из 5 прогонов.
   - V20 (часть P1, R9) — `SameNameUploadRaceTest.php`: два upload `x.pdf` в одну папку → ровно один узел `x.pdf`, вторая задача
     `failedWith(NodeNameConflictException::class)`; нет 500 и нет второго узла.
   - V21 — `LockOrderRaceTest.php`: 10 итераций `move(A → F)` ‖ `upload(в F)` → обе успешны в каждой итерации (deadlock не выходит
     наружу — максимум внутренний ретрай), дерево ацикличное, `used_bytes` = сумма голов.
   - SQLite: `packages/drive/tests/Feature/QuotaReserveTest.php` — `reserveBytes` при `quota_bytes = NULL` (без ограничения),
     ровно на границе (успех), +1 байт (исключение, `used_bytes` не изменился); копия поддерева сверх квоты → `QuotaExceededException`,
     строк копии нет.
8. `docs/guide/storage-and-blobs.md` и `docs/guide/uploads.md` — атомарная квота, порядок блокировок, сага promote;
   `CHANGELOG.md [Unreleased]` (Fixed: E2; AD21); golden `PublicApiSnapshotTest` (`BlobStore::commitStaged`).

**Scope Excluded:** `v_documents.revision_count` и номера ревизий документов (P4.1, R8); `OnNameConflict::Rename`
(«x (2).pdf») для V20 (P3.3); единый ingest с политикой/инспекторами (P3.7); `vaulter:drives:recount` (P7.2);
`revision_count`/`number` переименования (P2.1).

## 02-decisions.md — D21 — Конкурентность: атомарные инварианты и порядок блокировок · T0

- `02:823f7930`

**Решение.**

- `02:bab642e5`

- Квота: внутри транзакции
  `UPDATE v_drives SET used_bytes = used_bytes + :d WHERE id = :id AND (quota_bytes IS NULL OR used_bytes + :d <= quota_bytes)`;
  0 строк → `QuotaExceededException` и откат. Предпроверка остаётся только ради быстрого отказа.
  Семантика — байты головных ревизий (как сейчас).

- `02:20cf0d50` — _P1.6 — v_files.version_count; v_documents.revision_count — P4.1_

- Номер ревизии: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` строки `v_files`/`v_documents`, номер =
  `revision_count + 1`, delta квоты — от **перечитанной** головы.

- `02:dbbbb7c4`

- Канонический порядок блокировок: drive (tree lock) → узлы по возрастанию id → агрегат
  (file/document) → CAS advisory lock `(scope_key, sha256)`. Любое Action, берущее больше
  одной блокировки, следует порядку (arch-ревью + тест deadlock-сценария на PG).

- `02:add00ef0`

- `VaulterDatabase::transaction()` повторяет транзакцию на `40001`/`40P01` (PG) и deadlock
  MySQL до 3 раз.

- `02:dbd5319b`

- Promote объекта в хранилище не входит в SQL-транзакцию: документированная сага
  «stage → promote → metadata commit → при откате объект-сирота → reconciler после grace».

## 08-data-model-and-migration.md — 5. Порядок блокировок (D21)

- `08:bd2319dc`

```
1. VaulterDatabase::lockTree(drive)            — только операции, меняющие структуру (move, restore, purge поддерева, delete drive)
2. SELECT … FOR UPDATE узлов по возрастанию id  — узел операции и назначение
3. SELECT … FOR UPDATE агрегата                 — v_files / v_documents (ревизии, голова)
4. UPDATE v_drives … used_bytes (условный)      — квота
5. VaulterDatabase::advisoryLock("cas:{scope_key}:{sha256}") — commit blob
```

- `08:e141b7f4`

Операция не берёт блокировку уровня N после блокировки уровня M > N. Тест на PG: два параллельных сценария с
пересекающимися ресурсами завершаются без deadlock или с одним ретраем.

diff --git a/docs/guide/storage-and-blobs.md b/docs/guide/storage-and-blobs.md
index 688b857..cc2c200 100644
--- a/docs/guide/storage-and-blobs.md
+++ b/docs/guide/storage-and-blobs.md
@@ -208,11 +208,29 @@ $blobStore->detach($fileVersion, $blob);   // bytes are NOT deleted here — GC
 $stream = $blobStore->read($blob);         // resource|null over the canonical bytes
 ```
 
-In the Drive, this is wired by `Vaulter\Drive\Actions\UploadFileAction`: it probes
-the size, enforces the storage quota *before* committing bytes, calls
-`BlobStore::commit()` inside a transaction, then creates the `File` / `FileVersion`
-and the edge. The `tenant_id` and `storage_id` handed to `commit()` are read
-straight off the owning `Storage` row.
+In the Drive, `UploadFileAction` prepares and hashes bytes through
+`ContentStore::stage()` before entering its SQL transaction. Inside the transaction,
+locks follow **drive → nodes by id → file aggregate → quota → CAS**. A revision
+reads its previous head and `version_count` from the locked file; its new number is
+`version_count + 1` and its quota delta is new size minus that head's size.
+
+`StorageStoreRepository::reserveBytes()` applies a conditional atomic UPDATE:
+`used_bytes = used_bytes + delta` only when the quota is unlimited or the result
+fits `quota_bytes`. A failed reservation throws `QuotaExceededException` and rolls
+back all metadata. Copies reserve the sum of their subtree's heads in one UPDATE.
+`used_bytes` includes heads in the trash; only purge frees those bytes.
+
+The upload then calls `BlobStore::commitStaged($staged, $scope, $disk)`, creates the
+file/version/edge and updates the head. `commitStaged()` leaves the staged object
+intact. Its caller discards it in `finally`, after completion or failure, so SQL
+serialization/deadlock retries (up to three attempts) reuse the same prepared bytes.
+`commit($stream, $scope, $disk)` remains the convenience stage/commitStaged/discard
+entry point. Custom `BlobStore` implementations must implement `commitStaged()`.
+
+Promotion is an idempotent storage operation outside SQL rollback: **stage →
+promote → metadata commit**. A failure after promotion can leave canonical bytes
+without a blob row. Reconciliation collects these orphans after the configured
+grace period under the same CAS lock, preserving objects referenced by winners.
 
 ## Blob lifecycle
 
diff --git a/docs/guide/uploads.md b/docs/guide/uploads.md
index e9c5e34..b94cda0 100644
--- a/docs/guide/uploads.md

@@ -9,16 +9,33 @@ outline: deep
 Large files upload **resumably**: a long upload survives a dropped connection, a page reload,
 even a different client picking up where the last one left off. Vaulter ships two resumable
 transports plus a one-shot direct path, and every one of them funnels the assembled bytes
-through the **same race-free `BlobStore.commit`** — so dedup, the race-free blob state
+through the **same race-free `BlobStore.commitStaged`** — so dedup, the race-free blob state
 machine and quota all apply, and the `sha256` is **recomputed server-side** from the assembled
 bytes (the client `sha256` / S3 ETag is **never** the content address).
 
 ::: info H.3 invariant
 The server never trusts a client-supplied hash. A declared `expected_sha256` is an integrity
-*hint*, not the content address — `BlobStore.commit` re-hashes the assembled staged file at
-commit time.
+*hint*, not the content address — `ContentStore.stage` hashes the assembled bytes before SQL commit.
 :::
 
+## Atomic quota and revisions
+
+Uploads prepare their bytes once before the commit transaction. The transaction
+locks drive → nodes by ascending id → file → quota → CAS. It reserves a positive
+head-size delta with a conditional UPDATE, so simultaneous writers cannot exceed
+`quota_bytes`. An unlimited quota is `null`; an exact-boundary reservation succeeds.
+The preliminary check is only a fast rejection for new files.
+
+A new revision reads the locked file's current head and counter, allocates
+`version_count + 1`, and accounts for the difference from that actual head. Smaller
+heads release space; trashed heads keep using space until purge. A subtree copy
+reserves all copied head bytes together or rolls back the whole copy.
+
+The transaction retries serialization failures/deadlocks up to three attempts,
+reusing the staged object, then discards it in `finally`. If SQL fails after physical
+promotion, the bytes form an orphan collected by `vaulter:reconcile-blobs --orphans
+--prune` after grace. Reconciliation keeps committed winners' bytes.
+
 ## Transports
 
 `UploadProtocol` (`Vaulter\Enums\Uploads\UploadProtocol`) selects the transport per session:
@@ -50,7 +67,7 @@ Initiated → Uploading → Assembled → Committed
 | `initiated` | The row exists; no bytes received yet (`received_bytes = 0`). |
 | `uploading` | At least one tus `PATCH` / S3 part has landed; `received_bytes` is the authoritative server-side offset. |
 | `assembled` | All bytes are present on the staged temp; ready to commit. |
-| `committed` | The assembled bytes went through `BlobStore.commit`; the `File` + `FileVersion` + node were created and `assembled_blob_id` is set. **Terminal.** |
+| `committed` | The assembled bytes went through `BlobStore.commitStaged`; the `File` + `FileVersion` + node were created and `assembled_blob_id` is set. **Terminal.** |
 | `aborted`   | The client (or a `DELETE`) abandoned the session; staged temp + S3 multipart cleaned. **Terminal.** |
 | `expired`   | The sweeper reclaimed a stale session past its TTL. **Terminal.** |

+    public function __construct(private readonly VaulterDatabase $database) {}
+
+    /** Reserve head bytes atomically inside the caller's transaction. */
+    public function reserveBytes(Storage $storage, int $delta): void
+    {
+        if ($delta <= 0) {
+            $this->adjustUsedBytes($storage, $delta);
+
+            return;
+        }
+
+        $connection = $this->database->connection();
+        $grammar = $connection->getQueryGrammar();
+        $table = $grammar->wrapTable($storage->getTable());
+        $key = $grammar->wrap($storage->getKeyName());
+        $updated = $connection->update(
+            "update {$table} set used_bytes = used_bytes + ? where {$key} = ? and (quota_bytes is null or used_bytes + ? <= quota_bytes)",
+            [$delta, $storage->getKey(), $delta],
+        );
+
+        $storage->refresh();
+
+        if ($updated === 0) {
+            throw QuotaExceededException::forStorage(
+                storageId: $storage->id,
+                quotaBytes: $storage->quota_bytes ?? 0,
+                usedBytes: $storage->used_bytes,
+                incomingBytes: $delta,
+            );
+        }
+    }

+    public function commitStaged(StagedObject $staged, BlobScope $scope, ?string $disk = null): Blob
+    {
+        $disk ??= Config::defaultDisk();
+        $storageKey = $this->content->keyFor(scope: $scope, sha256: $staged->sha256());
+
+        return $this->database->transaction(function () use ($staged, $scope, $disk, $storageKey): Blob {
+            $this->lock->acquire(scope: $scope->key(), sha256: $staged->sha256());
+
+            $result = $this->blobs->findOrCreate(
+                sha256: $staged->sha256(),
+                sizeBytes: $staged->sizeBytes(),
+                storageKey: $storageKey,
+                disk: $disk,
+                scope: $scope,
+                tenantId: $scope->tenantId(),
+                storageId: $scope->storageId(),
+            );
+
+            // Promotion is idempotent; a rollback leaves bytes for reconciliation.
+            if ($result['created'] || !$this->content->exists($result['blob']->storage_key, $result['blob']->disk)) {
+                $this->content->promote(staged: $staged, key: $result['blob']->storage_key, disk: $result['blob']->disk);
+            }
+
+            return $result['blob'];
+        });
+    }
+
     /**
      * @param array{role?: string, ordinal?: int, offset_bytes?: int|null, length_bytes?: int|null} $attributes
      */
diff --git a/packages/media/tests/Cas/BlobCommitAtomicityTest.php b/packages/media/tests/Cas/BlobCommitAtomicityTest.php
index fe81611..d59dcd2 100644
--- a/packages/media/tests/Cas/BlobCommitAtomicityTest.php
+++ b/packages/media/tests/Cas/BlobCommitAtomicityTest.php
@@ -98,3 +98,24 @@ function stringStream(string $bytes)
 
     return $stream;
 }
+
+it('commitStaged keeps the temp file reusable across an outer SQL rollback', function (): void {
+    $content = app(ContentStore::class);
+    $staged = $content->stage(stringStream('reusable staged bytes'));
+    $scope = BlobScope::drive('staged-test');
+    try {
+        expect(fn () => app(VaulterDatabase::class)->transaction(function () use ($staged, $scope): void {
+            app(BlobStore::class)->commitStaged($staged, $scope);
+            throw new RuntimeException('metadata rollback');
+        }))->toThrow(RuntimeException::class, 'metadata rollback');
+        expect(Blob::query()->count())->toBe(0)
+            ->and(is_file($staged->path()))->toBeTrue();
+        $blob = app(BlobStore::class)->commitStaged($staged, $scope);
+        expect(is_file($staged->path()))->toBeTrue()
+            ->and(stream_get_contents(app(BlobStore::class)->read($blob)))->toBe('reusable staged bytes');
+        expect(app(BlobStore::class)->commitStaged($staged, $scope)->id)->toBe($blob->id);
+    } finally {
+        $staged->discard();
+    }
+    expect(is_file($staged->path()))->toBeFalse();
+});


# COMPLETE FILE AGENTS.md
```
# Vaulter — инструкции для агентов

Vaulter — PHP-монорепозиторий пакетов Laravel. Работай только в затронутом пакете
`packages/<name>/`; не меняй соседние пакеты и не перезаписывай чужие изменения.

Используй только нативно доступные навыки SKM: компактный marketplace-профиль задан в
`.swissknife.json` (как CoreX). Локальный `.agents/skills/` — только CORE (см.
`scripts/sync-agent-skills.sh`); не держи полный php-package vendor. Не загружай
справочники, планы или навыки «на всякий случай» — выбирай их по задаче.

MCP-серверы `maind`, `codegraph` и `perplexity` настроены для scope
`project:vaulter`. Проверяй состояние `maind health --cwd` при изменении среды или
симптомах, а не перед каждой обычной правкой.

Перед изменением кода прочитай ближайшие проектные инструкции и существующие тесты.
Проверяй затронутый пакет; не объявляй пропущенную или упавшую проверку успешной.
Пуш, публикация, удаление данных и перезапись несвязанных изменений требуют явного
запроса владельца.

```


# COMPLETE FILE CHANGELOG.md
```
# Changelog

All notable changes to **Vaulter** will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- `BlobStore::commitStaged()` for quota-first uploads and reusable staging across SQL retries (AD21).
- Lossless `BlobScope`, `scope_key`, upgrade migration №7 and resumable
  `vaulter:blobs:repair-scopes --dry-run|--execute` (AD20).
- Batch ACL evaluation (`AccessResolver::evaluateMany`, `AclFolder`), `NodePage`,
  `ExactListing`, signed query-bound cursors and `listing.scan_budget` (AD12).
- Independent `ids.host_keys` configuration (default `varchar(64)`), `HostKeyColumns`
  and `VaulterSchema::hostKey()` for host-owned keys (N02, AD25).
- `InvalidDestinationException` (409 `invalid_destination`), `NodeNotTrashedException`
  (409 `node_not_trashed`), `InvalidNodeNameException` (422 `validation_failed`) and
  `Vaulter\Support\NodeName::normalize()` for tree and name invariants (AD13).
- `VaulterDatabase::lockTreeShared()`: the shared mode of the drive tree lock, taken by
  creates, uploads, copies and renames (AD13, D21).

### Changed
- CAS commit and content-key contracts take `BlobScope`; UUID/ULID scopes use
  NULL-free uniqueness and the same `cas:{scope_key}:{sha256}` lock in commit, GC
  and reconcile. Existing blob paths and disks remain authoritative (AD20).
- Children, search, trash, shared, folder children and Filament now display only nodes
  with effective View for every driver. Shared uses cursors instead of page/total;
  REST listings omit totals and reuse batch ACL results for abilities (AD12).
- `vaulter-documents` and `vaulter-filament` require `vaulter-drive` at runtime (AD02).
- HTTP routes use separate `routes.middleware.authenticated`, `public` and `hub`
  stacks; protected routes default to `auth:sanctum`. Legacy `middleware` overrides
  only the authenticated stack, and metrics are appended to all stacks (AD31).
- Domain events carry a readonly `eventId` ULID with an optional final constructor
  argument for replay. Webhook `event_id` now identifies an event occurrence rather
  than its resulting state; retries retain the same id (AD22).
- Domain HTTP errors include a stable `code` through `ProblemException` (AD29).
  Name conflicts now return 409 `node_name_conflict` (previously 422); assembled
  upload MIME mismatches return 422 `upload_rejected` with `reason: mime_mismatch`
  (previously 415). Existing validation fields and tus headers are preserved.
- `vaulter-azgard` now follows the public AzGuard 0.3 line and its widened
  `BackedEnum` panel/role contracts; CI checks out the latest public AzGuard commit
  directly from GitHub without a private repository token.

### Fixed
- Atomic storage quota reservations for uploads and subtree copies (E2, AD21),
  fresh head-size deltas, file revision counters under `FOR UPDATE`, canonical
  drive/node/file/quota/CAS lock order and three-attempt transaction retries.
- A node created with no parent (notably documents) is placed under the drive root
  instead of becoming a hidden second root; upgrade migration №6 moves existing second
  roots under their root (renaming clashes ` (legacy N)`, logged as `node.moved`) and
  adds a unique one-root-per-drive index. The repair is irreversible (N04, AD13).
- Create, upload, move, copy and restore only target live folders of the same drive,
  checked under row locks; the root can no longer be renamed, moved, copied, trashed
  or deleted; moves take the drive tree lock so concurrent moves cannot form a cycle;
  `forceDelete` only purges trashed nodes and node names are normalized (N05, AD13).
- Host owner, subject, author, tenant and actor columns now use `ids.host_keys`
  instead of Vaulter's entity-ID strategy. Upgrade migration №3 preserves existing
  values and restores the strategy's types on rollback (N02, AD25).
- Repository metadata, exception documentation, collaboration errors and install
  star prompt now point to `axiomasoft/vaulter`; package vendor names stay
  `axioma-studio/vaulter-*` (N20/E6, AD02).
- Filament Move filters destinations and checks workspace creation and destination
  Create access at action time, notifying the actor on denial (N14).
- Vaulter resource resolution no longer registers global `node`/`storage` bindings
  that interfere with host routes. Missing resources return their 404 problem code (N10, AD31).
- Outbox deduplication no longer loses repeated rename or trash/restore operations
  returning to the same state; only a replay of the same event id is deduplicated (E8).
- All domain exceptions now return their documented 4xx status instead of HTTP 500
  on JSON requests and Vaulter routes (N11, AD29); configuration and infrastructure
  errors retain Laravel's default handling.
- Vaulter transactions, CAS/tree locks, raw queries and after-commit callbacks now use
  `vaulter.connection` through the new `Vaulter\Database\VaulterDatabase` singleton,
  preserving atomicity when the host default connection differs (N01, AD25).
  UploadSession, UploadPart, NodeComment and NodeDocumentVersion also resolve that
  connection so their writes cannot autocommit outside those transactions.
- Release workflows now keep the root npm lockfile, test the npm workspace before
  publishing, pass the PHP split token through the action's supported environment,
  reject tags whose exact SHA lacks the ordinary CI checks, and publish the GHCR
  image under the source repository owner.

## [0.2.0] — 2026-07-15

### Changed
- **Mutation testing moved off Infection onto native Pest mutation, with real thresholds
  everywhere** (VLT-CANON Open risk #4 / VLT-REMED D38). Every package lane —
  `documents`, `drive`, `media`, `filament` — carried an Infection step under `|| true`, and
  **none of them had ever executed a single mutant**: Infection 0.33.3 ships no Pest adapter
  (its `testFramework` enum is `phpunit`/`phpspec`/`codeception`/`testo`), so it could not map
  this suite's tests to mutants at all. `|| true` reported that as green. The root lane had
  already moved to `pest --mutate` for `vaulter-core`; the four package lanes now follow.
  Each has its own mutation job with a coverage driver and a **real threshold with no
  `|| true`** — Pest exits non-zero below it. `--min=95` (= `floor(Score) - 5`) from a measured
  baseline: `core` and all four packages score **100.00%** (documents 1472 mutants, drive 1569,
  media 380, filament 189). The four `infection.json5` files are gone, `infection/infection` is
  out of every `require-dev`, and the `mutate` composer scripts plus `make mutate` (which still
  invoked the `vendor/bin/infection` removed in P5.9) now call Pest.
- **Re-platformed into an embeddable virtual document storage** (#20). The document/folder
  model gave way to a **Drive** domain — storages and a recursive node tree (folders/files) —
  backed by a content-addressable blob store (CAS). Collaboration became **node-attached** and
  standardised on **Hocuspocus** (Reverb is now for notifications, not collaboration). The
  release artifacts are the six split Composer packages `vaulter-core`, `vaulter-media`,
  `vaulter-drive`, `vaulter-documents`, `vaulter-filament` and `vaulter-azgard`.
- **`@vaulter/vue` (npm) ships outside this git tag, on its own track** (D3). The monorepo git
  tag `v0.2.0` is a Composer/PHP versioning event covering the six packages above; the Vue client
  (`@vaulter/vue`, `0.0.0`) versions on its independent npm cycle and is not part of this
  release's frozen public surface.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0, MAJOR): `NodeGrantCreated`, `NodeGrantRevoked`, `NodeShareCreated` and
  `NodeShareRevoked` are no longer deferred to commit** (security remediation P0.2 / F-P1.7-03).
  All four events dropped `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit` and are now dispatched **synchronously
  inside the grant/share mutation's database transaction**, so the outbox and activity rows stage
  atomically with the mutation — closing a crash window where a committed grant/share could lose
  its outbox/activity row. Consequence for listeners: a listener on any of these four events now
  runs **inside the still-open transaction**. It **must be synchronous and idempotent**; a
  `ShouldQueue` listener, or one that assumes an after-commit guarantee, can now run against a
  mutation that is later rolled back, or race the transaction outright. Drive queued or
  after-commit side-effects off the durable outbox/webhook relay instead of off these events
  directly. See `UPGRADE.md` for migration guidance.

### Removed
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the CAS engine moved out of `vaulter-core` into the new standalone package
  `axioma-studio/vaulter-media`** (P2 #5, D2/D3). The concrete content-addressed blob engine —
  `Sha256ContentStore`, `FlysystemStorageDriver`, `BlobStore`, `BlobGarbageCollector`, `BlobReconciler`,
  `StagedTempObject` — and the `vaulter:gc-blobs` / `vaulter:reconcile-blobs` commands moved from
  `Vaulter\Cas\*` to `Vaulter\Media\Cas\*` / `Vaulter\Media\Console\*`. Hosts referencing those FQCNs
  directly, or relying on `vaulter-core` alone for Drive file upload/download/versioning, must now also
  `require axioma-studio/vaulter-media`. `Contracts\Cas\*`, the `v_blobs` schema, `Models\Cas\Blob`,
  `Enums\Cas\BlobStatus` and `Repositories\Cas\BlobStoreRepository` remain in core. Config paths
  (`vaulter.cas.*`, `vaulter.dedup.*`) are unchanged. Dependency direction is one-way (media → core; no cycle).
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the file/folder tree, storage manager and resumable uploads moved out of
  `vaulter-core` into the new standalone package `axioma-studio/vaulter-drive`** (P2 #5, D1). The
  Drive Actions (`TrashNodeAction`, `UploadFileAction`), `DriveContext`, `DriveManager`,
  `StorageManager`, the `vaulter:purge-trash` / `vaulter:sweep-uploads` commands, Drive events
  (`FileUploaded`, `NodeCopied`, `NodeCreated`, `NodeMoved`, `NodeRenamed`, `NodeTrashed`,
  `NodeVersionCommitted`, `StorageCreated`, …), the Drive/Storage/Upload HTTP controllers, requests
  and resources, and the whole `Uploads\*` subtree (tus + S3-multipart handlers, upload session/part
  models and repositories) moved from `Vaulter\*` to `Vaulter\Drive\*`. Hosts referencing those FQCNs
  directly, or relying on `vaulter-core` alone for `Vaulter::for()`/`forStorage()`, node CRUD, uploads
  or downloads, must now also `require axioma-studio/vaulter-drive`.
  `Contracts\Drive\{DriveHandle,DriveHandleFactory}`, `Support\DriveNameRules`,
  `Exceptions\Nodes\NodeNameConflictException`, `Http\Resources\Drive\NodeResource` and the
  `v_nodes`/`v_upload_sessions`/`v_upload_parts` schemas remain in core.
  `vaulter.models.{upload_session,upload_part}` are no longer defaulted by core and now
  guard-throw when the drive package is absent; other config paths are unchanged. Dependency
  direction is one-way (drive → core+media; no cycle).
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): document editing, version history, comments and realtime collaboration
  moved out of `vaulter-core` into the new standalone package `axioma-studio/vaulter-documents`**
  (P2 #5, D2). The document Actions (`CreateDocumentNodeAction`, `UpdateDocumentContentAction`,
  `CreateNamedVersionAction`, `RestoreDocumentVersionAction`, `AddNodeCommentAction`,
  `ResolveNodeCommentAction`, `DeleteNodeCommentAction`, `PersistYjsSnapshotAction`), the default
  `Access\HostUserResolver`, the `Collaboration\*` drivers (`HocuspocusNodeCollaborationDriver`,
  `ReverbNodeCollaborationDriver`, `NodeCollaborationDriverManager`, `CollaborationCall`,
  `CollaborationCallResolver`), the document handlers (`TipTapHandler`, `PreviewHandler`,
  `DownloadOnlyHandler`, `UniverHandler`), the `HtmlExporter` / `MarkdownExporter`, the
  `NodeComment` / `NodeDocumentVersion` models and the document/collaboration/comment HTTP
  controllers moved from `Vaulter\*` to `Vaulter\Documents\*`. Hosts referencing those FQCNs
  directly, or relying on `vaulter-core` alone for document editing, versioning, comments or
  collaboration, must now also `require axioma-studio/vaulter-documents`.
  The handler-seam contracts (`FileHandler`, `DocumentContent`, `DocumentExporter`,
  `HandlerRegistry`, `NodeHandlerResolver`), the value objects (`FileDescriptor`,
  `HandlerCapabilities`, `HandlerUi`, `HandlerDescriptor`, `TipTapContent`,
  `OpaqueDocumentContent`) and the `v_node_comments`/`v_node_document_versions` schemas remain
  in core. **Their namespaces were subsequently renamed in the same unreleased cycle** — see the
  kind-neutral-rename entry below for the FQCNs a consumer actually lands on
  (`Contracts\Handlers\*` / `ValueObjects\Handlers\*`).
  `vaulter.models.{node_comment,node_document_version}` are no longer defaulted by core and now
  guard-throw when the documents package is absent; `vaulter.exporters` defaults to `[]` in core
  (`vaulter.handlers` has since gained a core-owned baseline — see VLT-CANON P0.4 below).
  Dependency direction is one-way (documents → core; no cycle) — `vaulter-drive` is not a runtime
  dependency of `vaulter-documents`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the format-handler contract is kind-neutral now — `DocumentHandler` →
  `FileHandler`, `Contracts\Documents\*` → `Contracts\Handlers\*`** (VLT-CANON P0.1, D7). The
  contract describes ANY file (image/pdf/download-only), not only editable documents, so
  "Engine" / "Document" naming was misleading for the built-in `DownloadOnlyHandler` /
  `PreviewHandler` fallbacks. Renamed: `Contracts\Documents\DocumentHandler` →
  `Contracts\Handlers\FileHandler`; `Contracts\Documents\{HandlerRegistry,NodeHandlerResolver,
  DocumentContent,DocumentExporter}` moved to `Contracts\Handlers\*` (same class names);
  `ValueObjects\Documents\DocumentCapabilities` → `ValueObjects\Handlers\HandlerCapabilities`;
  `ValueObjects\Documents\{HandlerDescriptor,HandlerUi,FileDescriptor}` moved to
  `ValueObjects\Handlers\*` (same class names). `ValueObjects\Documents\{TipTapContent,
  OpaqueDocumentContent,LinkSchemeValidator}` were unaffected by this item — they have since
  moved to `vaulter-documents` in P0.3 (see below). The npm package `@vaulter/vue` renamed the mirrored TS type
  `DocumentCapabilities` → `HandlerCapabilities` (`types.ts`); `HandlerDescriptor`/`HandlerUi`
  are unchanged. `config('vaulter.handlers')` / `config('vaulter.exporters')` keys are
  unaffected. See `UPGRADE.md` for the FQCN migration table.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the `DocumentExporter::export()` parameter WIDENS to the opaque
  `DocumentContent` (was the concrete `TipTapContent`)** (VLT-CANON P0.2). The contract no
  longer assumes TipTap. Callers are unaffected (a `TipTapContent` is a `DocumentContent`);
  **implementations break**: an exporter still declaring `export(TipTapContent $content)` is
  no longer signature-compatible and fatals on class load. A custom exporter must widen its
  parameter to `Vaulter\Contracts\Handlers\DocumentContent` and narrow internally to the
  concrete shape it renders — the built-in `HtmlExporter` / `MarkdownExporter` narrow to
  `TipTapContent` and throw the new
  `Vaulter\Exceptions\Documents\UnsupportedDocumentContentException` otherwise. See
  `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): TipTap's content shapes moved out of `vaulter-core` into
  `vaulter-documents`** (VLT-CANON P0.3). `core` declares only the kind-neutral `DocumentContent`
  contract; it must not know any handler's concrete shape. Moved (same class names, new FQCN
  root `Vaulter\Documents\*`): `ValueObjects\Documents\{TipTapContent,LinkSchemeValidator,
  OpaqueDocumentContent}` → `Documents\ValueObjects\*`; `Attributes\TiptapExtensions` →
  `Documents\Attributes\TiptapExtensions`; `Exceptions\Documents\InvalidTipTapContentException` →
  `Documents\Exceptions\InvalidTipTapContentException`. The store-time allow-list, read-time
  decay and URL-scheme scrub (F-P1.11-01/-02, R-01/R-11/R-12) are unchanged byte-for-byte. A
  core-only host (no `vaulter-documents` installed) is unaffected. `config('vaulter.tiptap.*')`
  stays in core (host-facing config, not a TipTap shape). See `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the format-handler registry ENGINE moved from `vaulter-documents` to
  core** (VLT-CANON P0.4, D10 — superseding H.5 / the T5 Option-B topology allocation).
  `DocumentHandlerRegistry`, `NodeHandlerResolver`, `DocumentFileDescriptorFactory`, the
  `Capability` enum and the baseline `PreviewHandler`/`DownloadOnlyHandler` moved to
  `Vaulter\Engines\*` / `Vaulter\Enums\Handlers\Capability` (same class names, new FQCN root); a
  drive-only host with no `vaulter-documents` installed now resolves a REAL preview/download
  handler descriptor for every node instead of a silent `null` degrade.
  `Vaulter::registerHandler()` / `Vaulter::handlers()` no longer throw `MissingPackageException`
  when `vaulter-documents` is absent — the seam is core-owned and unconditional now.
  `vaulter-documents` keeps only its own format additions (`TipTapHandler`, the feature-flagged
  `UniverHandler`). The baseline `PreviewHandler`/`DownloadOnlyHandler` are also registered under
  the container tag `'vaulter.handlers'`, so a host carrying a pre-P0.4 published config
  (`'handlers' => []`, which wins the shallow config merge) does not silently lose preview. See
  `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): `v_node_document_versions.format` no longer carries a schema
  `default('tiptap')`; the autosave and Yjs-snapshot writers now stamp the format explicitly**
  (VLT-CANON P0.4). `UpdateDocumentContentAction` and `PersistYjsSnapshotAction` previously let
  the DB default fill `format` when appending a NEW version row to an existing document — so a
  **markdown or univer document's autosave rows were stamped `'tiptap'` regardless of its real
  format**. They now carry the head version's format forward. The value reaches clients through
  `NodeDocumentVersionResource.format` and the version-history API: **a client branching on
  `format === 'tiptap'` must be updated.** `CreateNamedVersionAction`'s no-head fallback likewise
  changed from the literal `'tiptap'` to `DocumentType::RichText->value` (`'richtext'`). No
  backfill ships — pre-existing rows keep their stored value; `format` is an advisory label, the
  handler is always resolved from the node's mime (D4). See `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): `HandlerCapabilities::$richText` now defaults to `false` (was `true`)**
  (VLT-CANON P0.5). A kind-neutral handler makes no rich-text claim unless it opts in. The two
  built-in editable handlers are unaffected in behaviour (`TipTapHandler` now declares
  `richText: true` explicitly; `UniverHandler` already declared `richText: false`); a
  third-party handler relying on the implicit `true` default must now set it explicitly.
- **`NodeExportService::contentOf()` is now kind-neutral** (VLT-CANON P0.5, closing a P0.2/P0.3/
  P0.4 open finding). A non-rich-text node's export now returns its own `OpaqueDocumentContent`
  shape instead of decaying to an empty TipTap document — `HtmlExporter`/`MarkdownExporter`'s
  existing narrowing guard now correctly rejects it with `UnsupportedDocumentContentException`
  instead of silently rendering blank content. `CreateDocumentNodeData::$format`'s default
  changed from the invalid literal `'tiptap'` to `DocumentType::RichText->value` (`'richtext'`).
  See `UPGRADE.md`.
- **The AzGuard bridge is named `axioma-studio/vaulter-azgard`, not
  `axioma-studio/vaulter-permissions-azgard`** (VLT-CANON P2.1, final structural canon). The
  package directory moved `packages/permissions-azgard/` → `packages/azgard/` and the namespace
  `Vaulter\Permissions\Azgard\*` → `Vaulter\Azgard\*`. The core-owned contract seam
  `Vaulter\Contracts\Access\AzgardGuard` is unaffected — only the bridge package's own name and
  namespace changed. **Note this is not a released-package break:** the bridge has never been
  published (the repo carries no tag, and the split/Packagist pipeline is tag-triggered), so
  `vaulter-permissions-azgard` never existed on Packagist and the `Added` entry below lists the
  bridge under its final name. Only a host tracking this monorepo's dev branch — requiring the old
  package name via a path/VCS repo, or importing `Vaulter\Permissions\Azgard\*` FQCNs — has to
  update. See `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): catch-all folders resolved by role** (VLT-CANON P2.3, final structural
  canon). `documents/src/Documents/` (tautological name), the TipTap-only slice of
  `documents/src/Support/`, and the pure-helper slice of `drive/src/Uploads/` were dissolved into
  the domain-service folder/layer matching each class's actual role: `TipTapHandler`/`UniverHandler`
  → `Vaulter\Documents\Engines\Handlers`; `NodeExportService` → `Vaulter\Documents\Export`;
  `CollaborationSessionService` → `Vaulter\Documents\Collaboration`; `TipTapRenderer`/
  `HasTiptapExtensions` → `Vaulter\Documents\Support\TipTap(\Concerns)`; `MagicByteSniffer`/
  `StagedUpload`/`UploadModels` → `Vaulter\Drive\Support\Uploads`. Pure move, zero behaviour
  change. See `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): `documents/src/Access/HostUserResolver` moved to
  `Vaulter\Documents\Collaboration\HostUserResolver`** (VLT-CANON P2.4, final structural canon).
  `Access/` held a single class that wasn't an access-control class — it implements the
  collaboration webhook's `HostUserResolver` contract, not anything ACL-shaped — so it now sits
  next to the collaboration drivers it serves. core's `Access/` and `Webhooks/` domain-service
  folders were audited against ADR-0017 in the same pass and left unchanged (they conform). Pure
  move, zero behaviour change. See `UPGRADE.md`.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): the legacy `document_id`-bound DocVault path was excised**
  (ADR-0014 step 2), leaving a single canonical `v_nodes` document path (kind=document
  nodes with node-attached comments and versions). Removed: the `Document`,
  `DocumentFolder`, `DocumentVersion`, `DocumentPermission`, `DocumentComment` and
  `DocumentShare` models and their `v_document*` tables and migrations; the legacy
  document/folder/version/permission/comment/share repositories, controllers, Actions,
  events, resources and requests; the `Document`-bound collaboration driver
  (`Contracts\Collaboration\CollaborationDriver` + its Reverb/Hocuspocus implementations
  and `Vaulter::driver()`); the `Contracts\Access\PermissionResolver` seam
  (`Vaulter::resolvePermissionsWith()` / `permissionResolver()` and the
  `vaulter.permission_resolver` config key); and the
  `vaulter.models.{document,version,folder,permission,comment,share}` config entries with
  their table names. A dev-only, idempotent `drop_legacy_docvault_tables` migration drops
  any pre-existing `v_document*` tables. Use `Vaulter::for()` and the node/document
  endpoints instead.
- **BREAKING (pre-1.0): `@vaulter/vue` dropped the legacy document-centric surface**
  (ADR-0014 step 4), which called the removed `/documents`, `/folders`, `/documents/*`
  endpoints. Removed: the client namespaces `documents`, `versions`, `folders`,
  `permissions`, `shares` and legacy `collaboration`; the composables `useDocuments`,
  `usePermissions`, `useShares`, `useDocumentExport`, `useCollaboration`, `useVaulterEditor`;
  the components `VaulterFileManager`, `VaulterEditor`, `VaulterVersionHistory`,
  `VaulterPermissionsPanel`, `VaulterSharePanel`, `VaulterBreadcrumb`; and the legacy
  `createCollaborationSession` transport. Use the Drive surface instead — `client.nodes`/
  `useNodes`/`VaulterDrive`, `client.grants`/`useNodeGrants`, `client.nodeShares`/
  `useNodeShares`, `useDocumentNode`, `useNodeCollaboration`. `useComments` /
  `VaulterCommentsPanel` were repointed to the node comment API (`/nodes/{node}/comments`,
  prop `nodeId` instead of `documentId`).

### Added
- **`@vaulter/vue` explicit engine-component registry** (VLT-CANON P1.2) —
  `registerEngineComponent(id, component)` / `unregisterEngineComponent(id)` /
  `resolveEngineComponent(id)` / `registeredEngineComponentIds()` / `clearEngineComponents()`,
  formalising `VaulterDocumentNode`'s `ui.component`→editor mapping as a public, explicitly
  imported API (not an ambient global). `componentMap` stays fully supported and takes
  precedence; the registry is an additional global fallback for hosts with many mount points.
  In development builds, an unmapped handler now logs one `console.warn` per unique
  `ui.component`/`id`. Purely additive — no existing prop or behaviour changed.
- **Config-first Storage Panels** — `Vaulter::for()`, per-panel table prefixes and id strategy
  (`bigint` | `ulid` | `uuid7`), with a per-panel schema/migration generator.
- **Content-addressable blob store (CAS)** — scoped dedup (global/tenant/storage), race-safe
  commit (advisory-lock on Postgres), two-phase garbage collection and a reconcile command.
- **Composite ACL** — `AccessResolver` over node grants, host-role drivers and share links, with
  access-aware listing; **four permission drivers**: Database (default), Gate, Spatie and AzGuard.
- **Real-time collaboration (node-attached)** — Hocuspocus driver, Yjs snapshot persistence and a
  TipTap projection; JWT-authenticated rooms bound to a single node.
- **Resumable uploads** — tus + S3-multipart, server-recomputed content hashes, a sweep command.
- **Append-only activity feed** and **transactional-outbox webhooks** with a single relay-sweep
  owner and idempotent delivery.
- **Handler-aware documents** — rich-text (TipTap) and opaque snapshots (e.g. the Univer
  spreadsheet handler), plus a handler-aware create-path (`mime` resolves the node's handler).
- **AzGuard bridge** (`axioma-studio/vaulter-azgard`) — a real `AzgardGuard` adapter
  delegating to AzGuard scoped roles and workspace-context checks, keeping core AzGuard-free.
- **Vue client** (`@vaulter/vue`) — Drive components (folder-picker, no `window.prompt`),
  shared-with-me view, node-collaboration composables and the headless spreadsheet shell.
- **Full Postgres portability lane** running the schema-portable suite against real Postgres
  alongside the default in-memory SQLite.
- **Pluggable export formats**: register a `\Vaulter\Contracts\DocumentExporter` via
  `config('vaulter.exporters')` or `Vaulter::registerExporter()` to export documents to custom
  formats (PDF, DOCX, …) alongside the built-in HTML and Markdown. The export response now also
  returns the exporter's `mime_type`; binary exporters return base64-encoded `content`.
- **Swappable share-link token generator**: `config('vaulter.share_token_generator')` — implement
  `\Vaulter\Contracts\ShareTokenGenerator` to mint short, hashed or prefixed share tokens (the
  default stays a 48-char random string).
- **Configurable table prefix**: `config('vaulter.table_prefix')` (env `VAULTER_TABLE_PREFIX`,
  default `v_`) prefixes every Vaulter table. Published migrations and models resolve table names
  through it, so the package coexists with host tables and naming conventions.

```
# COMPLETE FILE docs/guide/storage-and-blobs.md
```
---
title: Storage & Blobs
description: How Vaulter stores file bytes — content-addressed blobs keyed by sha256, per-scope deduplication, the race-free two-phase garbage collector, and Flysystem disks.
outline: deep
---

# Storage & Blobs

Vaulter never copies file bytes into its own tables. Every uploaded file is stored
**once** as an immutable, content-addressed **blob** on a Flysystem disk, and the
database keeps only metadata: the `sha256`, the size, the disk, the scope-qualified
key, a lifecycle status, and the edges that pin the blob to file versions.

This is the **content-addressed store (CAS)**. It gives you transparent
deduplication, a clear separation between *bytes on disk* and *rows in the
database*, and a garbage collector that is safe to run while uploads are in flight.

::: info Where this lives
The CAS implementation is in `Vaulter\Media\Cas\*` (package `axioma-studio/vaulter-media`;
the host-facing `BlobLock` stays at `Vaulter\Cas\BlobLock` in core), the row model is
`Vaulter\Models\Cas\Blob`, the lifecycle enum is `Vaulter\Enums\Cas\BlobStatus`, and
configuration sits under the `cas`, `dedup`, `gc` and `storage` keys of
`config/vaulter.php`.
:::

## The mental model

```
upload stream
     │
     ▼
ContentStore::stage()   ─► local seekable temp file, sha256 recomputed from THOSE bytes
     │
     ▼
BlobStore::commit()     ─► one locked transaction: find-or-create v_blobs row by (scope, sha256)
     │                       └─ dedup hit?  reuse the row, skip the physical write
     ▼
ContentStore::promote() ─► write staged bytes to "<scope>/ab/<sha256>" on the disk
     │
     ▼
v_file_version_blobs    ─► the EDGE that pins the blob alive (liveness truth)
```

Three contracts split the work, and all three are swappable through `config('vaulter.cas')`:

| Contract | Default | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| `Vaulter\Contracts\Cas\BlobStore` | `Vaulter\Media\Cas\BlobStore` | High-level lifecycle: `commit` / `attach` / `detach` / `read`. The single CAS entry point. |
| `Vaulter\Contracts\Cas\ContentStore` | `Vaulter\Media\Cas\Sha256ContentStore` | Byte side: `stage` / `promote` / `get` / `exists` / `delete` / `keyFor`. Knows bytes, hashes and keys — never a `v_blobs` row. |
| `Vaulter\Contracts\Cas\StorageDriver` | `Vaulter\Media\Cas\FlysystemStorageDriver` | Thin Flysystem abstraction: `put` / `get` / `exists` / `delete` / `temporaryUrl` / `disk`. |

```php
// config/vaulter.php
'cas' => [
    'content_store'  => \Vaulter\Media\Cas\Sha256ContentStore::class,
    'storage_driver' => \Vaulter\Media\Cas\FlysystemStorageDriver::class,
    'blob_store'     => \Vaulter\Media\Cas\BlobStore::class,
],
```

All three are bound in the service provider and resolved through the container, so
the values are config-cache safe (FQCNs only, no closures). Point any of them at
your own class to change how bytes are staged, hashed, promoted, or stored — see
[Recipes › Custom storage driver](/guide/recipes).

## Bytes on disk, metadata in the database

A blob is just metadata. The `v_blobs` row (model `Vaulter\Models\Cas\Blob`) carries:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `sha256` | The lowercase hex content address (always recomputed server-side). |
| `size_bytes` | Authoritative size of the durable bytes. |
| `disk` | The Flysystem disk the canonical object lives on. |
| `storage_key` | The scope-qualified path of the object — `"<scope>/ab/<sha256>"`. |
| `status` | Lifecycle state (see [Blob lifecycle](#blob-lifecycle)). |
| `ref_count` | A **reconciled cache** of the live edge count — *not* the liveness truth. |
| `dedup_scope` | `global` \| `tenant` \| `storage` — the boundary the address is unique within. |
| `scope_key` | Lossless `global`, `tenant:{id}` or `drive:{id}`; unique together with `sha256`. |
| `tenant_id` / `storage_id` | Informational scope IDs; `scope_key` owns deduplication. |
| `first_seen_at` / `committed_at` / `delete_after_at` | Staging, commit, and GC-grace timestamps. |

The bytes themselves never appear in any column. The model timestamps are disabled
(`public $timestamps = false`) — the CAS manages its own three timestamps.

### The storage key is scope-qualified

`ContentStore::keyFor(BlobScope $scope, string $sha256)` uses the first two digest
characters as one shard: `{scope-segment}/{sha[0:2]}/{sha}`. Historical scopes keep
`{legacy-segment}/{sha[0:2]}/{sha[2:4]}/{sha}`. Migration preserves their stored paths;
reads always use the blob row's `storage_key` and `disk`.

::: warning Why the scope segment matters
Under per-scope dedup, two scopes may legitimately hold the same `sha256`. Without
the scope segment baked into the key, *one* scope's garbage collector could delete
*another* scope's live bytes. The scope segment guarantees distinct scopes never
resolve to the same physical object.
:::

The scope segment is derived from the dedup scope:

- `global` → `g`
- `tenant` → `t-{id}`, or `g` when there is no tenant
- `storage` → `d-{id}`

`BlobScope` preserves string IDs, including UUIDs, ULIDs and leading zeroes.
Identifiers contain 1–96 bytes. An ID matching `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}` is used verbatim;
other IDs use `~` followed by unpadded base64url. `fromSegment()` reverses this
encoding and rejects noncanonical segments. Legacy factories retain `global`,
`s<id>`, `t<id>`, `snull` and `tnull` paths.

## Hashing: the staged file, never the live stream

`Sha256ContentStore::stage()` drains the incoming stream into a **local, seekable
temp file** (in 1 MiB chunks) and computes the `sha256` from *those durable bytes*.

::: danger Vaulter never trusts a client-supplied hash
The content address is **always** recomputed server-side from the staged file — a
lying client `sha256`, a resumable-upload `expected_sha256`, or an S3 multipart
`ETag` are never used as the content address. Hashing happens *outside* the database
transaction, so hashing a multi-GB upload never holds the row/advisory lock.
:::

The staged object (`Vaulter\Media\Cas\StagedTempObject`, implementing
`Vaulter\Contracts\Cas\StagedObject`) exposes `path()`, `sha256()`, `sizeBytes()`,
`readStream()` and an idempotent `discard()` that removes the temp file once the
bytes are promoted (or the commit rolls back).

## Deduplication & `dedup.scope`

The dedup scope decides the boundary a content address is unique within:

```php
// config/vaulter.php
'dedup' => [
    'scope' => env('VAULTER_DEDUP_SCOPE', 'tenant'), // global|tenant|storage
],
```

| Scope | Uniqueness | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `global` | `UNIQUE (scope_key, sha256)` | One physical object per `sha256` across the whole install. |
| `tenant` | `UNIQUE (scope_key, sha256)` | One object per tenant — no cross-tenant existence leak. **Default.** |
| `storage` | `UNIQUE (scope_key, sha256)` | One object per storage container. |

`BlobScope::forUpload()` selects a `scope_key` from this setting. The schema always
uses `UNIQUE(scope_key, sha256)`; scope changes do not alter that index shape.
`UNIQUE(storage_key)` remains an independent physical-address guard.

::: tip The UNIQUE index is the portable race backbone
When two requests upload identical bytes concurrently, the `UNIQUE (scope_key, sha256)`
constraint is the cross-database backbone: one `INSERT` wins, the loser catches the
unique violation, re-`SELECT`s the winning row, and reuses it. This holds on
PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQLite even where the advisory lock below is a no-op.
:::

::: warning Choosing a scope is a one-way door
The scope is stored in every blob's `scope_key` and `storage_key`. Pick
it at install time. `tenant` is the safe default for multi-tenant apps because it
prevents one tenant from learning another tenant holds a given file (an existence
leak), at the cost of storing identical bytes once per tenant. See
[Multi-tenancy](/guide/multi-tenancy) for how `dedup.scope = tenant` interacts with
host-owned tenancy.
:::

## The commit flow

`BlobStore::commit()` is the single CAS entry point. It runs **one** transaction
whose **first statement** is the per-`(scope, sha256)` lock, so every liveness
decision is made from rows read *after* the lock:

```php
// Vaulter\Media\Cas\BlobStore::commit() — simplified
$staged = $this->content->stage($stream);          // 1. stage + hash OUTSIDE the tx
$key    = $this->content->keyFor($scope, $staged->sha256());

$this->database->transaction(function () use (…) {
    $this->database->advisoryLock('cas:'.$scope->key().':'.$staged->sha256());          // 2. lock = statement #1

    $result = $this->blobs->findOrCreate(…);                  // 3. find-or-create by dedup key
                                                              //    (a tombstoned match is RESURRECTED)

    if ($result['created'] || !$this->content->exists($key)) { // 4. promote bytes only when needed
        $this->content->promote($staged, $key, $disk);
    }

    return $result['blob'];
});
```

Key properties (all from `Vaulter\Media\Cas\BlobStore`):

- **Staging happens outside the transaction.** Only the liveness decision is locked.
- **A dedup hit skips the physical write.** Bytes are promoted only when the row is
  newly created (or the canonical object is somehow missing — `promote()` is
  idempotent and conditional, so a resurrected row whose object vanished is
  re-promoted safely).
- **No synchronous `ref_count++`.** The liveness truth is the *edge* in
  `v_file_version_blobs`; `ref_count` is a reconciled cache that may lag.

### Attaching and reading

A committed blob is pinned alive by a **version↔blob edge**:

```php
$blobStore->attach($fileVersion, $blob, ['role' => 'data', 'ordinal' => 0]);
$blobStore->detach($fileVersion, $blob);   // bytes are NOT deleted here — GC handles that
$stream = $blobStore->read($blob);         // resource|null over the canonical bytes
```

In the Drive, `UploadFileAction` prepares and hashes bytes through
`ContentStore::stage()` before entering its SQL transaction. Inside the transaction,
locks follow **drive → nodes by id → file aggregate → quota → CAS**. A revision
reads its previous head and `version_count` from the locked file; its new number is
`version_count + 1` and its quota delta is new size minus that head's size.

`StorageStoreRepository::reserveBytes()` applies a conditional atomic UPDATE:
`used_bytes = used_bytes + delta` only when the quota is unlimited or the result
fits `quota_bytes`. A failed reservation throws `QuotaExceededException` and rolls
back all metadata. Copies reserve the sum of their subtree's heads in one UPDATE.
`used_bytes` includes heads in the trash; only purge frees those bytes.

The upload then calls `BlobStore::commitStaged($staged, $scope, $disk)`, creates the
file/version/edge and updates the head. `commitStaged()` leaves the staged object
intact. Its caller discards it in `finally`, after completion or failure, so SQL
serialization/deadlock retries (up to three attempts) reuse the same prepared bytes.
`commit($stream, $scope, $disk)` remains the convenience stage/commitStaged/discard
entry point. Custom `BlobStore` implementations must implement `commitStaged()`.

Promotion is an idempotent storage operation outside SQL rollback: **stage →
promote → metadata commit**. A failure after promotion can leave canonical bytes
without a blob row. Reconciliation collects these orphans after the configured
grace period under the same CAS lock, preserving objects referenced by winners.

## Blob lifecycle

`Vaulter\Enums\Cas\BlobStatus` is the state machine:

```
Pending → Committed → Tombstoned → Deleting → Deleted
              ↑____________|
             (resurrection)
```

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Pending` | Row exists, bytes staged but not yet durable+verified. A crashed staging leaves a `Pending` row swept on timeout. |
| `Committed` | Bytes durable, `sha256` verified — the live state. |
| `Tombstoned` | GC marked it unreferenced; `delete_after_at` carries the grace deadline. A concurrent commit **resurrects** it back to `Committed`. |
| `Deleting` | The sweep claimed it; the physical delete is in flight. |
| `Deleted` | Bytes gone; the row is kept as an idempotent tombstone so a re-run of the sweep is a no-op. |

The enum enforces legal transitions via `allowedTransitions()` / `canTransitionTo()`,
and `isLive()` returns true for `Pending` and `Committed` (both pin their bytes).

## Garbage collection (two-phase, race-free)

When you trash and purge a file, its version edges detach. A blob with no remaining
edges is *garbage* — but deleting its bytes safely, while uploads may be
resurrecting that exact `sha256`, is the hard part. Vaulter's GC
(`Vaulter\Media\Cas\BlobGarbageCollector`) solves it in two phases, **ACL-blind** and run
as the system (a node a user cannot see still pins its blob).

### Phase 1 — mark

Scan `committed ∧ ref_count <= 0` (the `scopeGarbageCollectable` query). For each
candidate, under the per-`(scope, sha256)` lock, **re-verify** that:

- the status is still `Committed` (the cheap scan may be stale), and
- no live edge exists in `v_file_version_blobs`.

If both hold, `tombstone()` sets `status = Tombstoned` and `delete_after_at = now +
grace`. The grace deadline is armed *here*, so the sweep can never delete within the
grace window.

### Phase 2 — sweep

Take `tombstoned` blobs past their grace (`scopeSweepable`). Under the lock,
re-verify the blob is still `Tombstoned`, still has no live edge, and is past
`delete_after_at`. Only then: `markDeleting` → physical delete of the
scope-qualified object → `markDeleted` (an idempotent tombstone; a missing object is
fine).

::: tip The safety invariant
Every transaction that changes a content address's liveness — upload-commit,
GC-mark, GC-sweep — takes the per-`(scope, sha256)` lock as its **first statement**
and decides from rows read *after* it. That is what makes online GC safe: a commit
that wins the lock resurrects a tombstoned blob *before* the sweep can see it; a
sweep that wins the lock re-checks edge existence *before* deleting.
:::

Per database driver (`Vaulter\Cas\BlobLock`):

- **PostgreSQL** — `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(scope||sha256))`, a real mutex
  held for the whole transaction. The sweep additionally runs `SERIALIZABLE` with
  retry-on-`40001` (up to 5 attempts).
- **MySQL / MariaDB** — no transaction-advisory lock that survives like PG's, so the
  store relies on `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the blob row; the advisory helper is a
  no-op.
- **SQLite** — single-writer; the transaction itself serialises.

The `UNIQUE (scope_key, sha256)` constraint is the portable backbone that holds on all
three even when the advisory lock is a no-op.

### Pending sweep

`sweepPending()` clears rows from a crashed staging — a `Pending` row older than the
grace window (`first_seen_at < now − grace`) that carries no edge. Its possibly
partial object is deleted and the row finalised as a deleted tombstone, under the
lock so a slow-but-live commit is never stolen.

### Running the collector

```bash
# Both phases (mark + sweep), grace from config('vaulter.gc.grace_hours'):
php artisan vaulter:gc-blobs

# Override the grace window (floored at 1h):
php artisan vaulter:gc-blobs --grace=48

# Also clear crashed-staging PENDING rows:
php artisan vaulter:gc-blobs --pending
```

The command prints a per-phase tally — `Tombstoned X, swept Y, skipped Z (grace Nh)`.

```php
// config/vaulter.php
'gc' => [
    'grace_hours'        => env('VAULTER_GC_GRACE_HOURS', 24),
    // The separate, generally more generous window for the orphan disk-walk
    // (see Reconciliation below). Defaults to gc.grace_hours, floored at 1h.
    'orphan_grace_hours' => env('VAULTER_ORPHAN_GRACE_HOURS', env('VAULTER_GC_GRACE_HOURS', 24)),
],
```

::: warning The grace window must outlast your slowest upload
A tombstoned blob can be resurrected by an in-flight upload of the same `sha256`. The
grace must be **≥ the worst-case multipart-commit latency** so an upload can
resurrect first. It is floored at 1h by `Config::gcGraceHours()` regardless of
config — never set it to zero expecting an immediate purge.
:::

::: details Schedule it yourself
`vaulter:gc-blobs` and `vaulter:reconcile-blobs` are registered by the package but
**not** auto-scheduled — Vaulter does not touch your scheduler. Add them where you
schedule maintenance, e.g. in `routes/console.php`:

```php
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schedule;

Schedule::command('vaulter:reconcile-blobs')->daily();
Schedule::command('vaulter:gc-blobs --pending')->dailyAt('03:00');
```

Run reconcile *before* GC so `ref_count` reflects reality when the mark scan reads it.
:::

See [Recipes › Blob GC & reconcile cron](/guide/recipes) for a full operations
recipe.

## Reconciliation: rebuilding the `ref_count` cache

`ref_count` is a cache, not the truth, so it drifts. `vaulter:reconcile-blobs`
(backed by `Vaulter\Media\Cas\BlobReconciler`) rebuilds it from the
`v_file_version_blobs` edge counts — the real liveness source — and reports
integrity hazards. It **never deletes anything**.

```bash
php artisan vaulter:reconcile-blobs
```

It walks every blob, rewrites any stale `ref_count`, and flags **dangling rows** — a
`Committed` blob whose canonical object is *missing on disk* (a half-promoted commit
or a botched manual delete). The command exits non-zero and warns with the offending
blob keys:

```
Reconciled 1240 blobs, corrected 3 ref_count caches.
Dangling rows (committed blob, missing object): 01J…ABC, 01J…XYZ
```

### Orphan objects: the disk walk

A dangling row is one of two inverse integrity hazards; the other is the **orphan
object** — bytes on disk that match the committed CAS key layout but have **no blob
row at all** (a commit that promoted its bytes then rolled the row back, or leaked
bytes). Detecting it requires walking the *disk*, the inverse of the row-side
reconcile above. The `--orphans` flag adds that disk-walk to the same command:

```bash
# Detect orphan objects (report-only) in addition to the row-side reconcile:
php artisan vaulter:reconcile-blobs --orphans

# Scan a specific disk and override the orphan grace window (hours, floored at 1h):
php artisan vaulter:reconcile-blobs --orphans --disk=s3 --orphan-grace=48

# DELETE the orphans found — PostgreSQL/MySQL only (refused on SQLite; see below):
php artisan vaulter:reconcile-blobs --orphans --prune
```

It prints its own line next to the reconcile tally:

```
Orphan walk (report-only): scanned 1240, found 2, pruned 0, skipped 1238 (disk s3, grace 24h).
Orphan objects (bytes with no blob row): t42/ab/cd/abcd…ef, t42/12/34/1234…99
```

::: warning Report-only by default — and the run FAILS when it finds orphans
Without `--prune` the walk only **detects and reports** orphans (a safe dry run on
every driver). Like the dangling-row check, a report-only run that finds any orphan
**exits non-zero** so cron/CI sees the hazard. Only a `--prune` run that actually
deleted them exits success.
:::

The sweep is built to *only ever* touch genuine canonical orphans. Its guards, in
order:

1. **Exact CAS-key layout, round-trip verified.** A path is a candidate only when it
   exactly matches `"<scope>/<a>/<b>/<sha256>"` *and* `keyFor(scope, sha256)`
   re-derives the **same** path — so the shard bytes are the real shard of that
   digest. Staging/tmp parts, resumable-upload fragments, foreign files and any
   mis-sharded path never match and are **never** considered for deletion.
2. **Any blob row means owned.** A matching object that has *any* `v_blobs` row (any
   status, looked up disk-agnostically since `storage_key` is globally unique) is
   owned, not an orphan.
3. **The orphan-grace window.** A row-less object is a deletable orphan only once it
   is **older than the orphan grace** — a just-promoted object may be a
   commit-in-progress whose row is not yet durable. Anything younger than the grace
   (or whose age the disk can't report) is skipped, never deleted.
4. **Locked recheck before delete.** A `--prune` delete happens under the same
   per-`(scope, sha256)` lock the GC uses, re-checking both row-absence and the grace
   *after* taking the lock, then deletes idempotently — so a commit that just created
   the owning row can never have its bytes swept out from under it.

::: danger `--prune` is refused on SQLite — PostgreSQL/MySQL only
The prune deletes bytes under a **write-less** critical section, which only serialises
against a concurrent commit on a driver whose lock orders a no-row-write section
(PostgreSQL advisory xact lock; MySQL `FOR UPDATE`). On **SQLite** that ordering never
engages — a concurrent commit could insert the owning row between the recheck and the
delete — so pruning is **refused** there (the command warns and degrades to
report-only). **Detection works on every driver**; only the destructive `--prune`
requires PostgreSQL/MySQL. Run report-only locally, or prune against the production DB.
:::

::: info A disk that can't be enumerated is skipped, not failed
The disk-walk needs a CAS content store that can list the disk (a `ListableContentStore`).
If the active store does **not** implement it, the walk reports `"listing unsupported"`,
scans nothing, and exits cleanly. A listable store over a backend that returns no entries
(for example a non-`FilesystemAdapter` disk that can't be enumerated) simply scans **zero**
objects — equally safe: nothing is enumerated, so nothing is deleted. Either way the
row-side reconcile result still stands.
:::

::: details The orphan grace is separate from the GC grace
The orphan walk uses `config('vaulter.gc.orphan_grace_hours')`
(env `VAULTER_ORPHAN_GRACE_HOURS`), which **defaults to `gc.grace_hours`** (24) and is
floored at 1h. Keep it ≥ your worst-case multipart-commit latency for the same reason
the GC grace must be generous: an in-flight resumable upload can promote its bytes long
before its row commits, and a fresh object must never be mistaken for an orphan.
:::

## Flysystem disks: `local`, S3, and per-storage

Blobs live on a standard Laravel Flysystem disk. The disk for a given blob is
resolved per call by `FlysystemStorageDriver::disk()`: a blob's own `disk` column
(set from the owning `v_storages.disk`) wins, otherwise the install default:

```php
// config/vaulter.php
'storage' => [
    'default_disk' => env('VAULTER_DISK', 'local'),
],
```

Because it is just a Flysystem disk, any driver works — `local`, `s3`, or a custom
one — configured in your app's `config/filesystems.php`. The CAS only does stream
writes plus a few object-store niceties that degrade gracefully:

- **Conditional create.** On an object store, `put()` with `ifNoneMatch` maps to
  `If-None-Match: *` so a concurrent uploader of identical bytes cannot clobber; a
  `412`/`409` is surfaced as a `false` return (re-`SELECT` + reuse), not an
  exception. On `local`/SQLite-test there is no conditional PUT, so it degrades to a
  best-effort `exists()` guard — the per-`(scope, sha256)` lock is the real
  serialiser there.
- **Server-side checksum.** On S3, `promote()` attaches
  `x-amz-checksum-sha256` (base64 of the raw digest) so S3 verifies the staged bytes;
  the local driver ignores it, so passing it is config-aware and safe.
- **Signed URLs.** `temporaryUrl()` mints a short-lived signed URL only on disks that
  support it (`providesTemporaryUrls()`); the `local`/SQLite-test disk returns
  `null` rather than throwing.

::: danger Keep the CAS bucket UNVERSIONED
The canonical CAS bucket must **not** use S3 lifecycle/versioning as a
blob-deletion mechanism. Vaulter's two-phase GC is the deletion authority; an S3
lifecycle rule deleting blobs out from under it will produce dangling rows. (A
lifecycle `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload` rule for *resumable uploads* is a
different, recommended backstop — see the `uploads` config.)
:::
