  key in one place.
- [Multi-tenancy](/guide/multi-tenancy) — how host-owned tenancy feeds `tenant_id`
  into blobs and why `dedup.scope = tenant` matters.
- [Recipes › Custom storage driver](/guide/recipes) — swap the disk / drive
  a custom `ContentStore`.
- [Recipes › Blob GC & reconcile cron](/guide/recipes) — production operations.

## Repairing historical mixed scopes

Migration №7 fills `scope_key` from historical IDs without moving any object.
Blobs previously committed without a numeric storage or tenant ID become
`legacy:snull` or `legacy:tnull`. Inspect them with
`php artisan vaulter:blobs:repair-scopes --dry-run`, then apply with `--execute`.
The report shows blob, digest, source scope, storage, edge count, target and action.
Unreferenced blobs and SHA-256 mismatches are reported without changing their bytes
or references. Each target runs in its own `VaulterDatabase` transaction: lock the
affected trees and both CAS addresses, verify source bytes, copy to the target,
verify target bytes, redirect revision edges and the legacy `blob_id`, and rebuild
ref-count caches. Existing target blobs retain their authoritative disk/path.
Old objects remain until GC. Interrupted repairs resume without duplicate moves;
successes append JSON to `storage/logs/vaulter-repair-scopes.log`.

The CAS saga is stage → SHA-256 → `cas:{scope_key}:{sha256}` lock → row lookup or
resurrection → conditional promotion → edge → SQL commit. SQL rollback after
promotion can leave an orphan. Reconcile recognizes both key layouts, waits for
`orphan_grace_hours`, and rechecks row absence under the same CAS lock before
removing bytes. GC uses the blob row's `scope_key` with that identical namespace.

```


# COMPLETE FILE docs/guide/uploads.md
```
---
title: Uploads
description: Resumable uploads — tus.io core on any disk, S3-style multipart on object stores, and a direct one-shot path, all funnelling through the same race-free CAS commit.
outline: deep
---

# Uploads

Large files upload **resumably**: a long upload survives a dropped connection, a page reload,
even a different client picking up where the last one left off. Vaulter ships two resumable
transports plus a one-shot direct path, and every one of them funnels the assembled bytes
through the **same race-free `BlobStore.commitStaged`** — so dedup, the race-free blob state
machine and quota all apply, and the `sha256` is **recomputed server-side** from the assembled
bytes (the client `sha256` / S3 ETag is **never** the content address).

::: info H.3 invariant
The server never trusts a client-supplied hash. A declared `expected_sha256` is an integrity
*hint*, not the content address — `ContentStore.stage` hashes the assembled bytes before SQL commit.
:::

## Atomic quota and revisions

Uploads prepare their bytes once before the commit transaction. The transaction
locks drive → nodes by ascending id → file → quota → CAS. It reserves a positive
head-size delta with a conditional UPDATE, so simultaneous writers cannot exceed
`quota_bytes`. An unlimited quota is `null`; an exact-boundary reservation succeeds.
The preliminary check is only a fast rejection for new files.

A new revision reads the locked file's current head and counter, allocates
`version_count + 1`, and accounts for the difference from that actual head. Smaller
heads release space; trashed heads keep using space until purge. A subtree copy
reserves all copied head bytes together or rolls back the whole copy.

The transaction retries serialization failures/deadlocks up to three attempts,
reusing the staged object, then discards it in `finally`. If SQL fails after physical
promotion, the bytes form an orphan collected by `vaulter:reconcile-blobs --orphans
--prune` after grace. Reconciliation keeps committed winners' bytes.

## Transports

`UploadProtocol` (`Vaulter\Enums\Uploads\UploadProtocol`) selects the transport per session:

| Protocol      | Value           | Works on | How |
| ------------- | --------------- | -------- | --- |
| **tus**       | `tus`           | **any** Flysystem disk | tus.io CORE — Creation + `HEAD`(`Upload-Offset`) + `PATCH`(`application/offset+octet-stream`). Each chunk is appended to a staged temp; `received_bytes` is the authoritative offset. |
| **S3 multipart** | `s3-multipart` | S3-style disks | Numbered parts recorded in `v_upload_parts` (re-upload of a part replaces it), stitched on complete. |
| **Direct**    | `direct`        | any | A one-shot non-resumable `POST` — the small-file fast path. |

The default is **tus** (the disk-agnostic resumable path). The transport is chosen at session
create time; the `UploadSessionManager` resolves the matching handler (`TusUploadHandler` /
`S3MultipartHandler`) from the container, so a host can swap either implementation.

## Session lifecycle

A resumable upload is a first-class row (`UploadSession`, `v_upload_sessions`). `UploadStatus`
drives the state machine:

```
Initiated → Uploading → Assembled → Committed
     └──────────┴──────────┘
            ↓ (abort / sweep)
       Aborted / Expired
```

| State       | Meaning |
| ----------- | ------- |
| `initiated` | The row exists; no bytes received yet (`received_bytes = 0`). |
| `uploading` | At least one tus `PATCH` / S3 part has landed; `received_bytes` is the authoritative server-side offset. |
| `assembled` | All bytes are present on the staged temp; ready to commit. |
| `committed` | The assembled bytes went through `BlobStore.commitStaged`; the `File` + `FileVersion` + node were created and `assembled_blob_id` is set. **Terminal.** |
| `aborted`   | The client (or a `DELETE`) abandoned the session; staged temp + S3 multipart cleaned. **Terminal.** |
| `expired`   | The sweeper reclaimed a stale session past its TTL. **Terminal.** |

Only `initiated` and `uploading` are **open** (accept bytes, sweepable). Transitions are
guarded by `UploadStatus::canTransitionTo` — a terminal state is never re-opened.

::: warning Feature gate
The resumable route group **and** the `v_upload_sessions` / `v_upload_parts` schema are gated
by the `uploads` feature flag (`config('vaulter.features.uploads')`, default `true`). The tus
endpoints are *additionally* gated by `config('vaulter.uploads.tus.enabled')` — a `403` when
disabled.
:::

## Targets

A session can target one of two shapes (it mirrors `UploadFileData`):

- **New file** → set `parent_id` (or omit it for the storage root) and a `name`; the commit
  creates a `kind=file` node under that folder.
- **New version** → set `node_id` (the existing `kind=file` node); the commit bumps
  `version_no` and repoints the head.

Authorization is per-target, enforced through the `AccessResolver` at every byte/state move
(both at create and on each subsequent op):

- **New file** → `canCreateInWorkspace($actor, $storage)` **and** `Ability::Create` on the
  destination folder.
- **New version** → `Ability::Edit` on the target node.

A denial is a `403` via `PermissionDeniedException`.

## Routes

All routes are mounted under the configured prefix (default `api/vaulter`) and require the
`uploads` feature. `{upload_id}` is the client UNIQUE handle (a plain scalar, **not**
route-model bound — the controller resolves the session and re-authorizes its target).

| Method   | Route                                        | Controller / action          | Purpose |
| -------- | -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | ------- |
| `POST`   | `/storages/{storage}/resumable-uploads`      | `TusUploadController@create` | Open a session (returns `Location` + `upload_id`). |
| `GET`    | `/resumable-uploads/{upload_id}`             | `TusUploadController@status` | Poll the session status. |
| `HEAD`   | `/resumable-uploads/{upload_id}`             | `TusUploadController@head`   | The authoritative `Upload-Offset` (resume point). |
| `PATCH`  | `/resumable-uploads/{upload_id}`             | `TusUploadController@patch`  | Append a chunk; on completion assemble→commit. |
| `DELETE` | `/resumable-uploads/{upload_id}`             | `TusUploadController@abort`  | Abort (staged temp + multipart reclaimed). |
| `POST`   | `/resumable-uploads/{upload_id}/parts`       | `S3MultipartController@parts`| Record (sign/proxy) a numbered part. |
| `POST`   | `/resumable-uploads/{upload_id}/complete`    | `S3MultipartController@complete` | Complete-multipart → assemble → commit. |

The direct one-shot path is the Drive upload endpoint
`POST /storages/{storage}/uploads` (a multipart form `file` + optional `parent_id` / `node_id`
/ `name`).

## tus core flow

The tus handler implements the three CORE operations directly (no heavy tus framework —
self-contained over Flysystem + the session table) and works on **any disk**.

1. **Create** — `POST /storages/{storage}/resumable-uploads`. The manager mints the session +
   `upload_id` (the tus `Location`) and stamps the TTL. The handler's `initialise()` is a no-op
   (the staged temp is created lazily on the first `PATCH`). Response `201` with
   `Tus-Resumable`, `Location`, `Upload-Offset: 0`.
2. **HEAD** — `head()` returns the **authoritative server-side `received_bytes`** as
   `Upload-Offset`, reconciled against the staged temp's real length (a crashed `PATCH` never
   reports an offset past the bytes that actually landed). A resuming client reads **this**,
   never its own guess.
3. **PATCH** — `patch()` appends the `application/offset+octet-stream` chunk at the declared
   `Upload-Offset`. The offset **must** match the server's current offset (a tus `409 Conflict`
   otherwise — the server, not the client, decides where the bytes go). When `received_bytes`
   reaches `total_size_bytes`, the controller drives `markAssembled` → `CommitUploadAction` and
   returns the created node (`201`).

::: code-group

```bash [curl]
# 1. Create the session (1 GiB file, into the storage root).
curl -i -X POST https://app.test/api/vaulter/storages/01J.../resumable-uploads \
  -H 'Authorization: Bearer <user-token>' \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"protocol":"tus","total_size_bytes":1073741824,"name":"video.mp4"}'
# → 201, Location: .../resumable-uploads/<upload_id>, Upload-Offset: 0

# 2. Ask the server where to resume from.
curl -I https://app.test/api/vaulter/resumable-uploads/<upload_id> \
  -H 'Tus-Resumable: 1.0.0' -H 'Authorization: Bearer <user-token>'
# → Upload-Offset: <server offset>

# 3. Send a chunk at that offset.
curl -X PATCH https://app.test/api/vaulter/resumable-uploads/<upload_id> \
  -H 'Tus-Resumable: 1.0.0' \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/offset+octet-stream' \
  -H 'Upload-Offset: 0' \
  -H 'Authorization: Bearer <user-token>' \
  --data-binary @chunk-0.bin
# → 204 Upload-Offset: <new>  (more chunks)  |  201 + node JSON (complete)
```

```vue [Vue]
<script setup lang="ts">
import { useUpload } from '@vaulter/vue'

const { uploadResumable, progress, sent, total, abort } = useUpload(storageId)

async function onPick(file: File) {
  // Drives the tus session chunk by chunk; progress is reactive.
  const node = await uploadResumable(file, {
    parentId,                       // or omit for the storage root
    onProgress: (s, t) => console.log(`${s}/${t}`),
  })
  console.log('committed node', node.id)
}
</script>

<template>
  <progress :value="progress" max="1" />
  <span>{{ sent }} / {{ total }}</span>
  <button @click="abort">Cancel</button>
</template>
```

:::

::: tip tus metadata
True tus clients can send an `Upload-Metadata` header (a comma-separated list of
`key base64(value)` pairs). The controller decodes it and merges it over the JSON-body
`metadata`, so both real tus clients and the JSON API are supported.
:::

## S3-multipart flow

When the session's disk is an object store, the bytes upload as **numbered parts**. The
handler records each part (`part_no` / `byte_offset` / `size` / `etag`) in `v_upload_parts` —
a re-upload of a part **replaces** its row (the S3 semantics, encoded in the
`(session, part_no)` composite primary key) — and on `complete` stitches the parts (ascending
by `part_no`) into the assembled staged temp.

```bash
# Record part 1 (the body is the part bytes).
curl -X POST 'https://app.test/api/vaulter/resumable-uploads/<upload_id>/parts?part_no=1&byte_offset=0' \
  -H 'Authorization: Bearer <user-token>' \
  --data-binary @part-1.bin
# → 201 { "part_no": 1, "size": ..., "etag": "\"...\"" }

# Complete → stitch → assemble → commit → returns the created node.
curl -X POST https://app.test/api/vaulter/resumable-uploads/<upload_id>/complete \
  -H 'Authorization: Bearer <user-token>'
```

::: info ETag is never the address
The per-part `etag` we store is the object-store handle only (for debug / the real
complete-multipart call) — it is **never** the content address. On `complete` the **stitched
bytes**, not the multipart ETag, are what `BlobStore.commit` re-hashes (H.3). The default
handler proxies parts through the `StorageDriver`, so the same flow works against a faked/local
disk in tests and a real S3 disk in production; a host wanting true presigned-PUT parts swaps
the handler — the record/complete contract is unchanged.
:::

## Assemble → CAS commit & dedup

`CommitUploadAction` is the **one place** staged bytes cross into the Drive, and it does so
through the same race-free path a one-shot upload uses:

```
staged temp → UploadFileData → UploadFileAction → BlobStore.commit
```

The whole thing runs in one outer transaction so a session is never half-committed:

1. The staged temp is read back as a stream (it lives on the session's disk under a `tmp/`
   prefix keyed by `upload_id`, so a multi-GB upload survives across requests).
2. `UploadFileAction` stages it locally, **recomputes the `sha256`**, and calls
   `BlobStore.commit` — dedup, the race-free state machine, ref-accounting and quota all apply.
   Two identical files commit to the **same blob** (content-addressed dedup); the session's
   `assembled_blob_id` is set to the blob the bytes deduped into — read from the committed
   version row, never from the client claim.
3. On success the session is marked `Committed`, and the staged temp is **discarded** (commit-
   or-abort cleanup) — done *outside* the transaction so a disk-delete hiccup never rolls back
   a committed file.

A premature `complete()` cannot skip ahead: `markAssembled` is a no-op unless the handler
reports every byte received.

## Abort & sweep

Two paths reclaim a session — both release the protocol-side state (S3
`AbortMultipartUpload` / per-part objects) and discard the staged temp:

- **Abort** (`DELETE /resumable-uploads/{upload_id}`) → terminal state `Aborted` (the client
  cancelled). Idempotent — a terminal session is returned untouched.
- **Sweep** (`php artisan vaulter:sweep-uploads`) → terminal state `Expired`. The command scans
  the **open** sessions (`initiated` | `uploading`) whose `expires_at` has elapsed and reclaims
  each. Schedulable.

```bash
# Reclaim stale sessions past their TTL (staged temp + S3 multipart cleaned).
php artisan vaulter:sweep-uploads
```

```php
// Schedule it (e.g. app/Console/Kernel.php or routes/console.php):
Schedule::command('vaulter:sweep-uploads')->hourly();
```

::: tip Pair with an S3 lifecycle rule
The sweeper aborts the in-flight multiparts it **knows about** (its `v_upload_parts` rows).
Pair it with an S3 bucket lifecycle rule `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload` as the backstop for
any orphaned multipart the table never saw — the lifecycle rule is *never* a blob-deletion
mechanism (the CAS bucket itself stays unversioned, H.3).
:::

## Configuration

```php
// config/vaulter.php
'uploads' => [
    // The hard ceiling on a session's declared total size (0 = ∞). Rejected at
    // initiate — an oversize upload never opens a session. env: VAULTER_UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES
    'max_bytes' => env('VAULTER_UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES', 5_368_709_120), // 5 GiB

    // The advertised default chunk size (a hint; the server still accepts any
    // PATCH/part size up to the remaining bytes). env: VAULTER_UPLOAD_CHUNK_SIZE
    'chunk_size' => env('VAULTER_UPLOAD_CHUNK_SIZE', 8_388_608), // 8 MiB

    // A session not committed within this window is swept (status → expired).
    // env: VAULTER_UPLOAD_SESSION_TTL_HOURS
    'session_ttl_hours' => env('VAULTER_UPLOAD_SESSION_TTL_HOURS', 24),

    // Gate the tus resumable endpoints (independent of the 'uploads' schema feature flag).
    // env: VAULTER_UPLOAD_TUS_ENABLED
    'tus' => [
        'enabled' => env('VAULTER_UPLOAD_TUS_ENABLED', true),
    ],
],
```

The `max_bytes` ceiling is enforced **twice**: up front at `initiate` (a declared total past
the ceiling never opens a session, so no bytes are staged that must be rejected) and again
mid-stream as the tus offset grows. The swappable models are bound via
`config('vaulter.models.upload_session')` and `config('vaulter.models.upload_part')`.

The one-shot binary Drive upload (`POST /storages/{storage}/uploads`) has a **separate**
policy block, `config('vaulter.drive')` — `max_upload_kb` (a per-file size ceiling, enforced
by `UploadFileRequest`) and `allowed_mime` (an optional mime allowlist; empty = permissive).
The `uploads` block above governs only the *resumable* sessions.

{
    "name": "axioma-studio/vaulter-core",
    "description": "Embeddable document management for Laravel — file manager, TipTap editor, version history and real-time collaboration, integrated through adapters.",
    "type": "library",

    "keywords": [
        "laravel",

        "collaboration",

# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Cas/BlobLock.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Cas;

use Vaulter\Database\VaulterDatabase;

/**
 * The per-(scope, sha256) liveness lock (H.3 safety invariant). EVERY transaction
 * that changes the liveness of a content address — upload-commit, GC-mark,
 * GC-sweep — takes this lock as its FIRST statement and makes every liveness
 * decision from rows read AFTER the lock. That is what makes online GC safe
 * against a concurrent re-commit (a commit that wins the lock resurrects a
 * tombstoned blob before the sweep can see it; a sweep that wins the lock
 * re-checks edge existence before deleting).
 *
 * Per driver:
 *  - pgsql : pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(scope||sha256)) — a real mutex held
 *            for the whole transaction, the GC-vs-upload backbone.
 *  - mysql : there is no transaction-advisory lock that survives like PG's, so we
 *            rely on SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the blob row inside the caller's
 *            transaction; this helper is a no-op and BlobStore locks the row.
 *  - sqlite: single-writer — the transaction itself serialises; no-op here.
 *
 * The UNIQUE (scope, sha256) constraint is the portable backbone that holds on
 * ALL three drivers even when this advisory lock is a no-op.
 */
final class BlobLock
{
    public function __construct(private readonly VaulterDatabase $database) {}

    /**
     * Take the per-(scope, sha256) advisory lock for the current transaction.
     * Must be called as the first statement inside $this->database->transaction().
     */
    public function acquire(string $scope, string $sha256): void
    {
        $this->database->advisoryLock('cas:'.$scope.':'.$sha256);
    }

    /**
     * Acquire multiple CAS keys in the database's lock order, including hash
     * collisions. Every operation needing multiple addresses uses this order.
     *
     * @param list<string> $keys full cas:{scope_key}:{sha256} keys
     */
    public function acquireMany(array $keys): void
    {
        if (!$this->isPostgres() || $keys === []) {
            return;
        }

        $this->database->connection()->select(
            'SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(lock_key) FROM '
            .'(SELECT DISTINCT hashtext(value) AS lock_key '
            .'FROM jsonb_array_elements_text(CAST(? AS jsonb)) ORDER BY lock_key) AS locks',
            [json_encode($keys, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR)],
        );
    }

    /**
     * Whether the active connection uses pessimistic row locks (FOR UPDATE) as the
     * lock mechanism — i.e. MySQL/MariaDB. PG uses the advisory lock above; SQLite
     * uses single-writer and ignores FOR UPDATE.
     */
    public function usesRowLock(): bool
    {
        return in_array($this->driver(), ['mysql', 'mariadb'], strict: true);
    }

    /**
     * Whether a critical section that takes this lock but performs NO database WRITE
     * is still serialised against a concurrent transaction by THIS driver.
     *
     * The orphan prune is exactly such a section (it re-reads under the lock, then
     * deletes BYTES off disk — no row write). It is safe only when the lock alone
     * orders it against a concurrent BlobStore::commit of the same content:
     *
     *  - pgsql : pg_advisory_xact_lock() is a real mutex held for the whole tx — a
     *            commit of the same (scope,sha256) blocks on it ⇒ serialised.
     *  - mysql : findByStorageKey(lock: true) issues SELECT … FOR UPDATE, so the
     *            recheck contends with the concurrent INSERT ⇒ serialised.
     *  - sqlite: acquire() is a no-op AND FOR UPDATE is ignored, so a read-only
     *            critical section never engages SQLite's single-writer lock — a
     *            concurrent commit can INSERT the owning row between the recheck and
     *            the byte delete. NOT serialised; the prune must be refused (the GC
     *            is unaffected — it does a real markDeleting() write that forces the
     *            write lock).
     */
    public function serializesCriticalSection(): bool
    {
        return $this->isPostgres() || $this->usesRowLock();
    }

    public function isPostgres(): bool
    {
        return $this->driver() === 'pgsql';
    }

    private function driver(): string
    {
        return $this->database->driver();
    }
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Contracts/Cas/BlobStore.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Contracts\Cas;

use Vaulter\Models\Cas\Blob;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\FileVersion;
use Vaulter\ValueObjects\Cas\BlobScope;

/**
 * The high-level content-addressed blob lifecycle (H.3): the race-free CAS.
 *
 * commit() is the single entry point — stage → hash the staged file →
 * find-or-create the blob by its per-scope dedup key inside ONE locked
 * transaction, resurrecting a tombstoned match and promoting bytes only when the
 * row is newly created (a dedup hit skips the physical write). There is NO
 * synchronous ref_count++ — liveness truth is the existence of an edge in
 * v_file_version_blobs, and ref_count is a reconciled cache.
 *
 * attach()/detach() manage those version↔blob edges.
 */
interface BlobStore
{
    /**
     * Stage the incoming stream, recompute its sha256, and find-or-create the
     * blob for the resolved dedup scope, returning the committed Blob row (its
     * bytes durable at the canonical key). Concurrency-safe: concurrent identical
     * uploads converge on one row via the UNIQUE (scope, sha256) backbone (PG
     * advisory lock first; MySQL SELECT…FOR UPDATE; SQLite single-writer).
     *
     * @param resource $stream
     */
    public function commit(mixed $stream, BlobScope $scope, ?string $disk = null): Blob;

    /** Commit prepared bytes without discarding them; the staging caller owns cleanup. */
    public function commitStaged(StagedObject $staged, BlobScope $scope, ?string $disk = null): Blob;

    /**
     * Create the version↔blob edge in v_file_version_blobs. The edge — not
     * ref_count — is the liveness truth (H.3).
     *
     * @param array{role?: string, ordinal?: int, offset_bytes?: int|null, length_bytes?: int|null} $attributes
     */
    public function attach(FileVersion $version, Blob $blob, array $attributes = []): void;

    /**
     * Remove a version↔blob edge. A blob with no remaining edges becomes a GC
     * candidate at the next reconciliation; the bytes are NOT deleted here.
     */
    public function detach(FileVersion $version, Blob $blob, string $role = 'data', int $ordinal = 0): void;

    /**
     * A read stream over a committed blob's canonical bytes, or null when absent.
     *
     * @return resource|null
     */
    public function read(Blob $blob): mixed;
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Contracts/Cas/ContentStore.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Contracts\Cas;

use Vaulter\ValueObjects\Cas\BlobScope;

/**
 * The low-level byte side of the CAS (H.3): stage an incoming stream to a local
 * seekable temp file (hashing those durable bytes server-side), then promote the
 * staged bytes to their canonical scope-qualified key. The high-level
 * find-or-create / dedup / GC lifecycle is BlobStore — ContentStore knows only
 * bytes, hashes and keys, never the v_blobs row.
 */
interface ContentStore
{
    /**
     * Drain the incoming stream into a local seekable temp file and recompute its
     * sha256 from the staged bytes (never the live stream / client hash / S3
     * ETag).
     *
     * @param resource $stream
     */
    public function stage(mixed $stream): StagedObject;

    /**
     * Promote staged bytes to the canonical CAS key on the given disk. Idempotent
     * and concurrency-safe: when the object already exists (another uploader of
     * the same bytes won) the promotion is a no-op rather than a fatal — the
     * driver uses If-None-Match: * where supported and treats 412/409 as "already
     * there". After promotion the staged temp file may be discarded.
     */
    public function promote(StagedObject $staged, string $key, ?string $disk = null): void;

    /**
     * A read stream over the canonical object at $key, or null when absent.
     *
     * @return resource|null
     */
    public function get(string $key, ?string $disk = null): mixed;

    public function exists(string $key, ?string $disk = null): bool;

    public function delete(string $key, ?string $disk = null): void;

    /**
     * Build "<scope>/ab/<sha256>" for new scopes, or preserve the legacy
     * "<scope>/ab/cd/<sha256>" layout for legacy scopes.
     * (H.3): the scope segment keeps two dedup scopes from ever sharing one
     * physical object, so one scope's GC can never delete another's live bytes.
     */
    public function keyFor(BlobScope $scope, string $sha256): string;
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Contracts/Cas/StagedObject.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Contracts\Cas;

/**
 * The result of staging an upload to a LOCAL, seekable temp file (H.3): the
 * sha256 is recomputed from these durable bytes, never from the live stream nor
 * a client-supplied hash nor an S3 multipart ETag. A StagedObject is the unit a
 * ContentStore hands to BlobStore — the bytes are not yet at their canonical CAS
 * key (that is promote()).
 *
 * It is read-only and self-cleaning: discard() removes the temp file once the
 * bytes have been promoted (or the commit rolled back).
 */
interface StagedObject
{
    /**
     * The absolute path of the local, seekable temp file holding the staged bytes.
     */
    public function path(): string;

    /**
     * The lowercase hex sha256 recomputed server-side from the staged temp file.
     */
    public function sha256(): string;

    public function sizeBytes(): int;

    /**
     * A read stream over the staged bytes (rewound), for promotion to the disk.
     *
     * @return resource
     */
    public function readStream(): mixed;

    /**
     * Remove the temp file. Idempotent — safe to call after promotion or on a
     * rolled-back commit.
     */
    public function discard(): void;
}

```

    public function timing(string $name, callable $operation, array $labels = []): mixed;
}

# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Database/VaulterDatabase.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Database;

use Closure;
use Illuminate\Database\Connection;
use Illuminate\Database\DatabaseManager;
use LogicException;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

final class VaulterDatabase
{
    public function __construct(private readonly DatabaseManager $db) {}

    public function connection(): Connection
    {
        return $this->db->connection(Config::connection());
    }

    public function driver(): string
    {
        return $this->connection()->getDriverName();
    }

    /**
     * @template TReturn
     *
     * @param  Closure(Connection): TReturn $callback
     * @param  int<1, max>                  $attempts
     * @return TReturn
     */
    public function transaction(Closure $callback, int $attempts = 3): mixed
    {
        return $this->connection()->transaction($callback, $attempts);
    }

    /**
     * The storage's tree lock, exclusive: for operations that change the shape of the
     * tree or touch many node rows (move, restore, trash, purge). Taken before any
     * node row lock.
     */
    public function lockTree(int|string $storageId): void
    {
        $this->requireTransaction('lockTree');

        if ($this->driver() === 'pgsql') {
            $this->advisoryLock('vaulter:tree:'.$storageId);

            return;
        }

        $this->connection()->table(Config::table('storages'))
            ->where('id', $storageId)->lockForUpdate()->first(['id']);
    }

    /**
     * The storage's tree lock, shared: for writes under a single locked parent
     * (create, upload, copy, rename). On PostgreSQL they run concurrently with each
     * other and wait only for an exclusive holder. Elsewhere the lock is the storage
     * row, which these writes update (quota) or reference (foreign-key check) anyway,
     * so it is taken exclusively.
     */
    public function lockTreeShared(int|string $storageId): void
    {
        $this->requireTransaction('lockTreeShared');

        if ($this->driver() === 'pgsql') {
            $this->connection()->statement('SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock_shared(hashtext(?))', ['vaulter:tree:'.$storageId]);

            return;
        }

        $this->lockTree($storageId);
    }

    public function advisoryLock(string $key): void
    {
        $this->requireTransaction('advisoryLock');

        if ($this->driver() === 'pgsql') {
            $this->connection()->statement('SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(?))', [$key]);
        }
    }

    public function afterCommit(Closure $callback): void
    {
        $this->connection()->afterCommit($callback);
    }

    private function requireTransaction(string $method): void
    {
        if ($this->connection()->transactionLevel() === 0) {
            throw new LogicException('[Vaulter] '.$method.'() must run inside VaulterDatabase::transaction().');
        }
    }
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Models/Cas/Blob.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Models\Cas;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsToMany;
use Illuminate\Support\Carbon;
use Vaulter\Enums\Cas\BlobStatus;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasExtraData;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasVaulterKeys;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\ResolvesVaulterConnection;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\FileVersion;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\FileVersionBlob;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * A content-addressable, immutable payload (master plan §2.3): identified by its
 * sha256 within a dedup scope and stored once on the Flysystem disk under a
 * scope-qualified storage_key. ref_count is a reconciled cache; the liveness
 * truth is the existence of a v_file_version_blobs edge (H.3). The byte handling
 * and GC commit-flow are M1b — this is the schema + model only.
 *
 * Swappable via config('vaulter.models.blob').
 *
 * @property int|string      $id
 * @property int|string|null $tenant_id
 * @property int|string|null $storage_id
 * @property string          $hash_algo
 * @property string          $sha256
 * @property int             $size_bytes
 * @property string          $disk
 * @property string          $storage_key
 * @property BlobStatus      $status
 * @property int             $ref_count
 * @property string          $scope_key
 * @property string          $dedup_scope
 * @property string|null     $etag
 * @property string|null     $md5
 * @property string|null     $crc32c
 * @property bool            $chunked
 * @property int|string|null $encryption_key_id
 * @property Carbon|null     $first_seen_at
 * @property Carbon|null     $committed_at
 * @property Carbon|null     $delete_after_at
 */
class Blob extends Model
{
    use HasExtraData;
    use HasVaulterKeys;
    use ResolvesVaulterConnection;

    public $timestamps = false;

    /**
     * Fully guarded (F-P1.5-04): every column is content-addressed or CAS
     * integrity state (sha256/storage_key are the content address itself;
     * ref_count/status drive GC). Mass-assigning ANY of them could point a CAS
     * reference at the wrong bytes. The sole writer, BlobStoreRepository, uses
     * forceFill() exclusively and is unaffected by this list.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    protected $guarded = ['*'];

    #[\Override]
    public function getTable(): string
    {
        return Config::table('blobs');
    }

    /**
     * Every file version that references this blob through the edge table.
     *
     * @return BelongsToMany<FileVersion, $this, FileVersionBlob>
     */
    public function versions(): BelongsToMany
    {
        return $this->belongsToMany(
            related: Config::fileVersionModel(),
            table: Config::table('file_version_blobs'),
            foreignPivotKey: 'blob_id',
            relatedPivotKey: 'file_version_id',
        )
            ->using(Config::fileVersionBlobModel())
            ->withPivot(['role', 'ordinal', 'offset_bytes', 'length_bytes']);
    }

    /**
     * GC-candidate scope (§2.3): committed blobs whose ref-count CACHE is zero.
     * That cache is rebuilt from the edge table by reconciliation, so a committed
     * blob reaches ref_count<=0 the moment its last edge is detached and
     * reconciled — that, not a pre-armed deadline, is what arms the mark scan.
     * The grace window is applied by tombstone() (mark) and enforced by sweep,
     * NOT here: requiring delete_after_at on a still-committed blob would gate the
     * scan on a column no commit path ever writes, so a genuinely orphaned blob
     * would never be tombstoned. This scope only narrows the scan — the real
     * liveness re-check happens under the per-(scope,sha256) lock (H.3).
     *
     * @param  Builder<$this> $query
     * @return Builder<$this>
     */
    public function scopeGarbageCollectable(Builder $query): Builder
    {
        return $query
            ->where('status', BlobStatus::Committed->value)
            ->where('ref_count', '<=', 0);
    }

    /**
     * Tombstoned blobs whose grace window has elapsed — the sweep candidates.
     *
     * @param  Builder<$this> $query
     * @return Builder<$this>
     */
    public function scopeSweepable(Builder $query): Builder
    {
        return $query
            ->where('status', BlobStatus::Tombstoned->value)
            ->whereNotNull('delete_after_at')
            ->where('delete_after_at', '<', now());
    }

    /**
     * @return array<string, string>
     */
    #[\Override]
    protected function casts(): array
    {
        return [
            'size_bytes' => 'integer',
            'ref_count' => 'integer',
            'status' => BlobStatus::class,
            'chunked' => 'boolean',
            'first_seen_at' => 'datetime',
            'committed_at' => 'datetime',
            'delete_after_at' => 'datetime',
        ];
    }
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Models/Concerns/HasVaulterKeys.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Models\Concerns;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Vaulter\Contracts\IdStrategy;
use Vaulter\Schema\IdStrategyResolver;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * Drives the default Vaulter models' key handling from the resolved IdStrategy
 * (ulid|uuid7|bigint). String, non-incrementing keys for ulid/uuid7; the
 * database assigns auto-increment bigint keys. Host apps that swap in their own
 * models (e.g. a tenant-aware BaseModel) simply omit this trait.
 */
trait HasVaulterKeys
{
    public static function bootHasVaulterKeys(): void
    {
        static::creating(static function (Model $model): void {
            if ($model->getKey() !== null) {
                return;
            }

            // The seam lives on the concrete model (this trait), not Model.
            $key = $model instanceof self ? $model->vaulterIdStrategyKey() : Config::idStrategy();
            $newId = app(IdStrategyResolver::class)->resolve($key)->newId();

            if ($newId === null) {
                return;
            }

            $model->setAttribute($model->getKeyName(), $newId);
        });
    }

    public function initializeHasVaulterKeys(): void
    {
        if ($this->vaulterIdStrategy()->modelConcern() === null) {
            return;
        }

        $this->keyType = 'string';
        $this->incrementing = false;
    }

    /**
     * Seam letting subclasses / panels override the strategy key (e.g. a
     * per-panel strategy in own-tables mode). Defaults to the global strategy.
     */
    protected function vaulterIdStrategyKey(): string
    {
        return Config::idStrategy();
    }

    private function vaulterIdStrategy(): IdStrategy
    {
        return app(IdStrategyResolver::class)->resolve($this->vaulterIdStrategyKey());
    }
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Models/Drive/File.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Models\Drive;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsTo;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\HasMany;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\SoftDeletes;
use Illuminate\Support\Carbon;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasExtraData;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasVaulterKeys;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\ResolvesVaulterConnection;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * The mutable 1:1 handle for a kind=file node (master plan §2.4): it owns the
 * version history and caches the head's mime/size. Swappable via
 * config('vaulter.models.file').
 *
 * @property int|string      $id
 * @property int|string|null $tenant_id
 * @property int|string      $node_id
 * @property int|string|null $current_version_id
 * @property string|null     $mime_type
 * @property int             $size_bytes
 * @property int             $version_count
 * @property int|string|null $created_by
 * @property Carbon|null     $created_at
 * @property Carbon|null     $updated_at
 * @property-read FileVersion|null $currentVersion
 */
class File extends Model
{
    use HasExtraData;
    use HasVaulterKeys;
    use ResolvesVaulterConnection;
    use SoftDeletes;

    /**
     * Closed guard-list (F-P1.8-02): id/tenant_id/node_id/current_version_id/
     * size_bytes/version_count/created_by/timestamps are structural or
     * server-computed bookkeeping — every write path that sets them already uses
     * forceFill() (FileStoreRepository). Only mime_type is a plain scalar left
     * mass-assignable.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    protected $guarded = [
        'id',
        'tenant_id',
        'node_id',
        'current_version_id',
        'size_bytes',

<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Models\Drive;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsTo;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsToMany;
use Illuminate\Support\Carbon;
use Vaulter\Models\Cas\Blob;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasExtraData;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasVaulterKeys;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\ResolvesVaulterConnection;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * An immutable file version (master plan §2.4): a monotonic version_no per file
 * pointing at a content-addressed blob (or a chunk manifest). The version ↔ blob
 * edges in v_file_version_blobs are the GC truth. Swappable via
 * config('vaulter.models.file_version').
 *
 * @property int|string      $id
 * @property int|string|null $tenant_id
 * @property int|string      $file_id
 * @property int             $version_no
 * @property int|string|null $blob_id
 * @property int|string|null $manifest_blob_id
 * @property string          $content_sha256
 * @property int             $size_bytes
 * @property string|null     $mime_type
 * @property string          $original_name
 * @property string          $status
 * @property bool            $keep_forever
 * @property int|string|null $created_by
 * @property Carbon|null     $created_at
 * @property Carbon|null     $committed_at
 */
class FileVersion extends Model
{
    use HasExtraData;
    use HasVaulterKeys;
    use ResolvesVaulterConnection;

    public const UPDATED_AT = null;

    /**
     * Closed allow-list (F-P1.8-02): id/tenant_id/file_id/version_no/blob_id/
     * manifest_blob_id/content_sha256/size_bytes/status/created_by/committed_at
     * are content-addressed or lifecycle columns — every write path that sets
     * them already uses forceFill()/create() with a server-built literal array
     * (FileVersionStoreRepository).
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    protected $fillable = [
        'mime_type',
        'original_name',
        'keep_forever',
    ];

    #[\Override]
    public function getTable(): string
    {

    }

     * @return BelongsTo<File, $this>

    }

    /**
     * The single-blob payload (when not chunked).
     *
     * @return BelongsTo<Blob, $this>
     */
    public function blob(): BelongsTo
    {
        return $this->belongsTo(Config::blobModel(), 'blob_id');
    }

    /**
     * Every blob this version references through the edge table — the GC truth.
     *
     * @return BelongsToMany<Blob, $this, FileVersionBlob>
     */
    public function blobs(): BelongsToMany
    {
        return $this->belongsToMany(
            related: Config::blobModel(),
            table: Config::table('file_version_blobs'),
            foreignPivotKey: 'file_version_id',
            relatedPivotKey: 'blob_id',

            ->using(Config::fileVersionBlobModel())

            'version_no' => 'integer',

# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Models/Drive/Node.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Models\Drive;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsTo;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\HasMany;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\SoftDeletes;
use Illuminate\Support\Carbon;
use Staudenmeir\LaravelAdjacencyList\Eloquent\HasRecursiveRelationships;
use Vaulter\Enums\Drive\NodeKind;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasExtraData;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\HasVaulterKeys;
use Vaulter\Models\Concerns\ResolvesVaulterConnection;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * A node in the unified folder|file|document adjacency tree (master plan §2.2,
 * the centre of the schema). One tree carries all kinds; move/rename are a single
 * parent_id/name UPDATE and reads use a recursive CTE (D5).
 *
 * The recursive relationships come from staudenmeir/laravel-adjacency-list; the
 * adjacency key column is parent_id and the local key is the strategy-driven
 * primary key (ulid|uuid7|bigint), so descendants()/ancestors() resolve the same
 * under every id strategy. Swappable — point config('vaulter.models.node') at a
 * subclass for tenant scoping.
 *
 * @property int|string      $id
 * @property string          $panel
 * @property int|string|null $tenant_id
 * @property int|string      $storage_id
 * @property int|string|null $parent_id
 * @property NodeKind        $kind
 * @property string          $name
 * @property string|null     $mime_type
 * @property string|null     $extension
 * @property int             $size_bytes
 * @property int|string|null $current_version_id
 * @property int|string|null $file_id
 * @property int             $position
 * @property bool            $is_pinned
 * @property bool            $inherit_grants
 * @property int|string|null $shortcut_target_id
 * @property int|string|null $owner_id
 * @property Carbon|null     $trashed_at
 * @property int|string|null $trashed_by
 * @property int|string|null $trashed_from_parent_id
 * @property Carbon|null     $created_at
 * @property Carbon|null     $updated_at
 * @property-read Storage          $storage
 * @property-read Node|null        $parent
 * @property-read File|null        $file
 * @property-read FileVersion|null $currentVersion
 */
class Node extends Model
{
    use HasExtraData;
    use HasRecursiveRelationships;
    use HasVaulterKeys;
    use ResolvesVaulterConnection;
    use SoftDeletes;

    /**
     * Closed allow-list (F-P1.8-02): only the handful of columns a caller
     * legitimately mass-assigns through fill()/create() with untrusted-shaped
     * arrays. Everything structural/security (id, tenant_id, storage_id,
     * parent_id, current_version_id, file_id, owner_id, shortcut_target_id, the
     * trashed_* triplet, timestamps) is excluded — every write path that sets
     * those columns already uses forceFill() (NodeStoreRepository) and is
     * unaffected by this list.
     *
     * @var list<string>
     */
    protected $fillable = [
        'name',
        'mime_type',
        'extension',
        'is_pinned',
        'inherit_grants',
    ];

    #[\Override]
    public function getTable(): string
    {
        // Honour an explicitly-set table (staudenmeir swaps it to the recursive
        // CTE alias during descendants()/ancestors()); only fall back to the
        // shared schema name when none was set.
        return $this->table ?? Config::table('nodes');
    }

    /**
     * The adjacency parent-key column (§2.2). Constant across id strategies — only
     * the column TYPE changes, not its name. Overrides the trait default.
     */
    public function getParentKeyName(): string
    {
        return 'parent_id';
    }

    /**
     * The local key the parent key points back at — the strategy-driven primary
     * key (id), so the recursive CTE joins on the right column under ulid/uuid7/
     * bigint alike. Overrides the trait default.
     */
    public function getLocalKeyName(): string
    {
        return $this->getKeyName();
    }

    /**
     * Cycle detection on READ (F-P4.2-02): a cycle in `parent_id` that reached
     * the data outside `MoveNodeAction::assertNoCycle()` (raw SQL, a seeder, a
     * legacy import, a future bulk-write path) must not send `ancestors()`/
     * `descendants()` into an inflated or unbounded recursive scan. The trait
     * defaults this to `false` — every Node overrides it `true`, so the
     * recursive CTE stops at the repeated row (a `not(is_cycle)` guard on the
     * recursive leg) on every adjacency-list read, not only through the Action.
     */
    public function enableCycleDetection(): bool
    {
        return true;
    }

    /**
     * Restrict a query (and any recursive descent built on it) to a single
     * storage's tree.
     *
     * @param  Builder<$this> $query
     * @return Builder<$this>
     */
    public function scopeTreeOf(Builder $query, int|string $storageId): Builder
    {
        return $query->where('storage_id', $storageId);
    }

    /**
     * @return BelongsTo<Node, $this>
     */
    public function parent(): BelongsTo
    {
        return $this->belongsTo(Config::nodeModel(), 'parent_id');
    }

    /**
     * @return HasMany<Node, $this>
     */
    public function children(): HasMany
    {
        return $this->hasMany(Config::nodeModel(), 'parent_id');
    }

    /**
     * @return BelongsTo<Storage, $this>
     */
    public function storage(): BelongsTo
    {
        return $this->belongsTo(Config::storageModel(), 'storage_id');
    }

    /**
     * @return BelongsTo<File, $this>
     */
    public function file(): BelongsTo
    {
        return $this->belongsTo(Config::fileModel(), 'file_id');
    }

    /**
     * @return BelongsTo<FileVersion, $this>
     */
    public function currentVersion(): BelongsTo
    {
        return $this->belongsTo(Config::fileVersionModel(), 'current_version_id');
    }

    /**
     * @return array<string, string>
     */
    #[\Override]
    protected function casts(): array
    {
        return [
            'kind' => NodeKind::class,
            'size_bytes' => 'integer',
            'position' => 'integer',
            'is_pinned' => 'boolean',
            'inherit_grants' => 'boolean',
            'trashed_at' => 'datetime',
        ];
    }
}

# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Repositories/Cas/BlobStoreRepository.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Repositories\Cas;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Illuminate\Database\UniqueConstraintViolationException;
use Vaulter\Cas\BlobLock;
use Vaulter\Enums\Cas\BlobStatus;
use Vaulter\Models\Cas\Blob;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;
use Vaulter\ValueObjects\Cas\BlobScope;

/**
 * Write-side row work for v_blobs (H.3). It owns the locked find-or-create by the
 * per-scope dedup key, resurrection of a tombstoned match, and the blob state
 * transitions. It opens NO transactions — BlobStore wraps every call in one — and
 * makes NO liveness decision beyond reading the row under the lock the caller
 * already took. The scope_key lookup mirrors the scope_sha_uniq index exactly.
 */
final readonly class BlobStoreRepository
{
    public function __construct(
        private BlobLock $lock,
    ) {}

    /**
     * Find-or-create the committed blob for a content address inside the caller's
     * already-locked transaction (H.3). Returns the existing row on a dedup hit
     * (resurrecting it if tombstoned) or a freshly created committed row, plus a
     * flag telling BlobStore whether bytes must be promoted (true ⇒ newly created,
     * promote; false ⇒ dedup hit, skip the physical write).
     *
     * UNIQUE (scope_key, sha256) is NULL-free. A savepoint around the INSERT
     * keeps PostgreSQL usable for the loser lookup after a unique violation.
     *
     * @return array{blob: Blob, created: bool}
     */
    public function findOrCreate(
        string $sha256,
        int $sizeBytes,
        string $storageKey,
        string $disk,
        BlobScope $scope,
        ?string $tenantId,
        ?string $storageId,
    ): array {
        $existing = $this->lockedLookup(sha256: $sha256, scope: $scope);

        if ($existing !== null) {
            $this->resurrectIfTombstoned($existing);

            return ['blob' => $existing, 'created' => false];
        }

        try {
            $blob = $this->query()->getConnection()->transaction(fn (): Blob => $this->insertCommitted(
                sha256: $sha256,
                sizeBytes: $sizeBytes,
                storageKey: $storageKey,
                disk: $disk,
                scope: $scope,
                tenantId: $tenantId,
                storageId: $storageId,
            ));

            return ['blob' => $blob, 'created' => true];
        } catch (UniqueConstraintViolationException) {
            // We lost the INSERT race (another committer of the same bytes won).
            // Re-SELECT under the same transaction and reuse its row — never treat
            // a unique violation as fatal (H.3). This is the loser branch of the
            // UNIQUE (scope, sha256) backbone.
            $winner = $this->lockedLookup(sha256: $sha256, scope: $scope);

            if ($winner === null) {
                throw new \RuntimeException("Lost the blob INSERT race for {$sha256} but the winning row is not visible.");
            }

            $this->resurrectIfTombstoned($winner);

            return ['blob' => $winner, 'created' => false];
        }
    }

    /**
     * Mark a committed, unreferenced blob as tombstoned with its grace deadline
     * (GC phase 1). Caller holds the per-(scope,sha256) lock and has re-verified
     * no live edge.
     */
    public function tombstone(Blob $blob, int $graceHours): void
    {
        $blob->forceFill([
            'status' => BlobStatus::Tombstoned,
            'ref_count' => 0,
            'delete_after_at' => now()->addHours($graceHours),
        ])->save();
    }

    /**
     * Claim a tombstoned blob for the sweep (Tombstoned → Deleting). Caller holds
     * the lock and re-verified the tombstone is still unreferenced past grace.
     */
    public function markDeleting(Blob $blob): void
    {
        $blob->forceFill(['status' => BlobStatus::Deleting])->save();
    }

    /**
     * Finalise the sweep (Deleting → Deleted) — an idempotent tombstone row; the
     * bytes are already gone. delete_after_at is cleared so the row drops out of
     * every sweep scan.
     */
    public function markDeleted(Blob $blob): void
    {
        $blob->forceFill([
            'status' => BlobStatus::Deleted,
            'delete_after_at' => null,
        ])->save();
    }

    /**
     * Overwrite the ref_count CACHE from a freshly counted edge total
     * (reconciliation). This is the only writer of ref_count — commit never
     * touches it (H.3, no hot-row).
     */
    public function setRefCount(Blob $blob, int $count): void
    {
        $blob->forceFill(['ref_count' => $count])->save();
    }

    /**
     * Look up the blob owning a canonical object by its storage_key — the
     * row-existence probe the orphan disk-walk runs for each on-disk object (bytes
     * with NO row are the orphan candidates). $lock takes the MySQL row lock so the
     * orphan reconciler can RE-CHECK under the per-(scope,sha256) advisory lock that
     * no row materialised since the cheap scan (a concurrent commit may have just
     * created it). Any status counts as "owned" — a committed/pending row pins its
     * bytes, a dangling/tombstoned row is the row-side reconcile/GC's job, never the
     * orphan sweep's.
     *
     * The probe is DISK-AGNOSTIC by default ($disk = null): storage_key is GLOBALLY
     * UNIQUE (its own single-column index, NOT a composite with disk), so a
     * canonical object has at most one owning row pinned to whatever disk THAT row
     * records — which may differ from the disk currently being scanned. Filtering by
     * the scan disk would read such a cross-disk-owned object as row-less and prune
     * its LIVE bytes. Pass an explicit $disk only when a disk-qualified probe is
     * genuinely intended.
     */
    public function findByStorageKey(string $storageKey, ?string $disk = null, bool $lock = false): ?Blob
    {
        $query = $this->query()->where('storage_key', $storageKey);

        if ($disk !== null) {
            $query->where('disk', $disk);
        }

        if ($lock && $this->lock->usesRowLock()) {
            $query->lockForUpdate();
        }

        return $query->first();
    }

    /**
     * Re-read a single blob FOR UPDATE on MySQL (the row lock that stands in for
     * PG's advisory lock); a plain reload elsewhere. Used by the GC to re-verify
     * state under the lock.
     */
    public function lockForUpdate(Blob $blob): Blob
    {
        $query = $this->query()->whereKey($blob->getKey());

        if ($this->lock->usesRowLock()) {
            $query->lockForUpdate();
        }

        /** @var Blob $fresh */
        $fresh = $query->firstOrFail();

        return $fresh;
    }

    /**
     * Look up the blob for a content address within its dedup scope, taking the
     * MySQL row lock when that driver is the lock mechanism.
     */
    private function lockedLookup(string $sha256, BlobScope $scope): ?Blob
    {
        $query = $this->query()->where('sha256', $sha256)->where('scope_key', $scope->key());
        if ($this->lock->usesRowLock()) {
            $query->lockForUpdate();
        }

        return $query->first();
    }

    private function insertCommitted(
        string $sha256,
        int $sizeBytes,
        string $storageKey,
        string $disk,
        BlobScope $scope,
        ?string $tenantId,
        ?string $storageId,
    ): Blob {
        // forceFill (P1.4/F-P1.5-04): Blob is intentionally fully guarded (CAS
        // integrity: sha256/storage_key are content-addressed) — this is the
        // sole trusted commit seam, never raw request input.
        $blob = new (Config::blobModel());
        $blob->forceFill([
            'tenant_id' => $scope->dedupScope() === 'storage' ? null : $tenantId,
            'storage_id' => $scope->dedupScope() === 'storage' ? $storageId : null,
            'hash_algo' => 'sha256',
            'sha256' => $sha256,
            'size_bytes' => $sizeBytes,
            'disk' => $disk,
            'storage_key' => $storageKey,
            'status' => BlobStatus::Committed,
            'ref_count' => 0,
            'dedup_scope' => $scope->dedupScope(),
            'scope_key' => $scope->key(),
            'committed_at' => now(),
        ]);
        $blob->save();

        return $blob;
    }

    /**
     * Resurrection (H.3): a commit that finds a tombstoned blob un-tombstones it
     * (→ committed, clear delete_after_at) in the same locked transaction. A
     * deleting/deleted row is also resurrected — its bytes will be re-promoted by
     * BlobStore since the canonical object may already be gone.
     */
    private function resurrectIfTombstoned(Blob $blob): void
    {
        if ($blob->status === BlobStatus::Committed || $blob->status === BlobStatus::Pending) {
            return;
        }

        $blob->forceFill([
            'status' => BlobStatus::Committed,
            'delete_after_at' => null,
            'committed_at' => $blob->committed_at ?? now(),
        ])->save();
    }

    /**
     * @return Builder<Blob>
     */
    private function query(): Builder
    {
        $model = Config::blobModel();

        return $model::query();
    }
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Repositories/Drive/FileStoreRepository.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Repositories\Drive;

use Vaulter\Models\Drive\File;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * Write-side row work for v_files — the mutable 1:1 handle for a kind=file node
 * (§2.4). Bare create + head/version-count bookkeeping. Opens NO transactions —
 * UploadFileAction wraps it.
 */
final class FileStoreRepository
{
    /** Read the aggregate and its revision counter under the caller's row lock. */
    public function lockForUpdate(File $file): File
    {
        $model = Config::fileModel();

        return $model::query()->whereKey($file->getKey())->lockForUpdate()->firstOrFail();
    }

    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $attributes
     */
    public function create(array $attributes): File
    {
        // forceFill (P1.4/F-P1.8-02): $attributes is a server-built literal array
        // from UploadFileAction/CopyNodeAction, never raw request input.
        $model = Config::fileModel();
        $file = new $model;
        $file->forceFill($attributes);
        $file->save();

        return $file;
    }

    /**
     * Repoint the file head to a new current version and refresh the cached
     * mime/size, bumping version_count (§2.4 commit flow).
     */
    public function repointHead(
        File $file,
        int|string $currentVersionId,
        ?string $mimeType,
        int $sizeBytes,
        int $versionNo,
    ): File {
        $file->forceFill([
            'current_version_id' => $currentVersionId,
            'mime_type' => $mimeType,
            'size_bytes' => $sizeBytes,
            'version_count' => $versionNo,
        ])->save();

        return $file;
    }
}

```


# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Repositories/Drive/FileVersionBlobStoreRepository.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Repositories\Drive;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Vaulter\Models\Cas\Blob;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\FileVersion;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\FileVersionBlob;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * Write-side row work for the v_file_version_blobs edge table — the GC liveness
 * truth (H.3). A blob is live iff an edge here references it, so attaching /
 * detaching an edge is what actually changes liveness; ref_count is only a
 * reconciled cache. Opens no transactions (the caller wraps it). Idempotent
 * upsert so a re-attach of the same (version, blob, role, ordinal) is a no-op.
 */
final readonly class FileVersionBlobStoreRepository
{
    /**
     * Create (or re-affirm) a version↔blob edge.
     *
     * @param array{role?: string, ordinal?: int, offset_bytes?: int|null, length_bytes?: int|null} $attributes
     */
    public function attach(FileVersion $version, Blob $blob, array $attributes = []): void
    {
        $role = $attributes['role'] ?? 'data';
        $ordinal = $attributes['ordinal'] ?? 0;

        $this->query()->updateOrInsert(
            [
                'file_version_id' => $version->getKey(),
                'blob_id' => $blob->getKey(),
                'role' => $role,
                'ordinal' => $ordinal,
            ],
            [
                'offset_bytes' => $attributes['offset_bytes'] ?? null,
                'length_bytes' => $attributes['length_bytes'] ?? null,
            ],
        );
    }

    /**
     * Remove a version↔blob edge. The blob's bytes are NOT deleted here — it just
     * becomes a GC candidate once reconciliation recomputes ref_count = 0.
     */
    public function detach(FileVersion $version, Blob $blob, string $role = 'data', int $ordinal = 0): void
    {
        $this->query()
            ->where('file_version_id', $version->getKey())
            ->where('blob_id', $blob->getKey())
            ->where('role', $role)
            ->where('ordinal', $ordinal)
            ->delete();
    }

    /**
     * Whether ANY edge still references this blob — the liveness re-check the GC
     * runs under the lock before it tombstones (H.3).
     */
    public function hasLiveEdge(Blob $blob): bool
    {
        return $this->query()->where('blob_id', $blob->getKey())->exists();
    }

    /**
     * Count the edges referencing this blob — the reconciled source of truth for
     * the ref_count cache.
     */
    public function countEdges(Blob $blob): int
    {
        return $this->query()->where('blob_id', $blob->getKey())->count();
    }

    /**
     * @return Builder<FileVersionBlob>
     */
    private function query(): Builder
    {
        $model = Config::fileVersionBlobModel();

        /** @var Builder<FileVersionBlob> $query */
        $query = $model::query();

        return $query;
    }
}

```

```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Repositories\Drive;

use Vaulter\Models\Drive\FileVersion;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;

/**
 * Write-side row work for the immutable v_file_versions (§2.4). Bare create +
 * the supersede transition for a prior head. Opens NO transactions —
 * UploadFileAction wraps it. version_no is monotonic per file (the unique index
 * is the backbone); the caller allocates numbers from the locked file counter.
 */
final class FileVersionStoreRepository
{
    /**
     * @param array<string, mixed> $attributes
     */
    public function create(array $attributes): FileVersion
    {
        // forceFill (P1.4/F-P1.8-02): $attributes is a server-built literal array
        // from UploadFileAction/CopyNodeAction, never raw request input.
        $model = Config::fileVersionModel();
        $version = new $model;
        $version->forceFill($attributes);
        $version->save();

        return $version;
    }

    /**
     * Mark the prior head version superseded (§2.4) when a newer version takes
     * over. The row stays — only the lifecycle status changes.
     */
    public function markSuperseded(FileVersion $version): FileVersion
    {
        $version->forceFill(['status' => 'superseded'])->save();

        return $version;
    }
}

# COMPLETE FILE packages/core/src/Repositories/Drive/NodeReadRepository.php
```
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Vaulter\Repositories\Drive;

use Illuminate\Contracts\Auth\Access\Authorizable;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Database\Query\Builder as QueryBuilder;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Collection;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Illuminate\Support\Carbon;
use Vaulter\Access\AccessResolver;
use Vaulter\Access\HostRoleDriverManager;
use Vaulter\Contracts\Access\GrantAuthoritativeDriver;
use Vaulter\Contracts\Observability\MetricsRecorder;
use Vaulter\Database\VaulterDatabase;
use Vaulter\Drivers\Permission\DatabasePermissionResolver;
use Vaulter\Enums\Access\GrantScope;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\Node;
use Vaulter\Models\Drive\Storage;
use Vaulter\Repositories\Access\GrantReadRepository;
use Vaulter\Support\Config;
use Vaulter\Support\Identity;
use Vaulter\Support\LoadTier;

/**
 * Read-side queries over the v_nodes adjacency tree (§2.2 / H.4). Tree reads use
 * the staudenmeir recursive relationships (descendants/ancestors), always bounded
 * by treeOf(storage) and — for bulk descents — withMaxDepth, never the BANNED
 * whereHas('descendants') / withCount('descendants') which break on MariaDB and
 * degrade on Postgres (H.4 arch-ban).
 *
 * queryForUser()/constrainToAccessible() narrow grant-authoritative drivers to
 * candidate IDs. Candidates require exact AccessResolver evaluation before display;
 * the REST listings and Filament table apply that after fetching each page.
 */
final class NodeReadRepository
{
    /**
     * The maximum recursion depth for bulk subtree descents — file managers live
     * at depth ≤ ~20 (§1.5), so this caps a pathological tree without an explicit
     * caller bound.
     */
    public const int DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH = 64;

    /**
     * Keep small sets bound, with room for storage, parent and cursor bindings even
     * on SQLite builds limited to 999 variables. Larger sets use escaped SQL literals
     * and add no bindings, regardless of the number of grant-reachable nodes.
     */
    private const int WHERE_IN_BIND_LIMIT = 900;

    public function __construct(
        private readonly AccessResolver $access,
        private readonly HostRoleDriverManager $drivers,
        private readonly GrantReadRepository $grants,
        private readonly MetricsRecorder $metrics,
    ) {}

    public function find(int|string $id): ?Node
    {
        return $this->query()->find($id);
    }

    /**
     * Every live (non-trashed, non-deleted) node in a storage's tree.
     *
     * @return Builder<Node>
     */
    public function forStorage(Storage $storage): Builder
    {
        return $this->query()
            ->treeOf($storage->id)
            ->whereNull('trashed_at');
    }

    /**
     * Direct children of a folder (or the storage root when $parent is null),
     * ordered by the manual sibling position then name.
     *
     * @return Builder<Node>
     */
    public function children(Storage $storage, ?Node $parent): Builder
    {
        return $this->metrics->timing(
            name: 'vaulter_tree_query_duration_seconds',
            operation: fn (): Builder => $this->query()
                ->treeOf($storage->id)
                ->whereNull('trashed_at')
                ->where('parent_id', $parent?->id)
                ->orderBy('position')
                ->orderBy('name')
                ->orderBy('id'),
            labels: [
                'query_kind' => 'children',
                'tier' => LoadTier::label(),
            ],
        );
    }

    /**
     * Whether the node is its drive's root — the one node without a parent, which
     * tree operations never take as an operand.
     */
    public function isRoot(Node $node): bool
    {
        $rootId = $node->storage->root_node_id;

        return $rootId !== null && Identity::stringify($node->getKey()) === Identity::stringify($rootId);
    }

    /**
     * The whole subtree below a node via the recursive CTE, bounded by
     * withMaxDepth so the descent never runs unbounded (H.4). The node itself is
     * NOT part of descendants() — callers add it when needed.
     *
     * @return Collection<int, Node>
     */
    public function descendants(Node $node, ?int $maxDepth = null): Collection
    {
        $model = Config::nodeModel();

        /** @var Collection<int, Node> $result */
        $result = $model::withMaxDepth(
            $maxDepth ?? self::DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH,
            static fn (): Collection => $node->descendants()->get(),
        );

        return $result;
    }

    /**
     * The node plus its whole subtree (the node first), bounded by withMaxDepth.
     * This is the unit a trash/restore/force-delete touches atomically.
     *
     * @return Collection<int, Node>
     */
    public function subtree(Node $node, ?int $maxDepth = null): Collection
    {
        return $this->metrics->timing(
            name: 'vaulter_tree_query_duration_seconds',
            operation: function () use ($node, $maxDepth): Collection {
                $model = Config::nodeModel();

                /** @var Collection<int, Node> $result */
                $result = $model::withMaxDepth(
                    $maxDepth ?? self::DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH,
                    static fn (): Collection => $node->descendantsAndSelf()->get(),
                );

                return $result;
            },
            labels: [
                'query_kind' => 'subtree',
                'tier' => LoadTier::label(),
            ],
        );
    }

    /**
     * @param  list<int|string>               $parentIds
     * @return array<string, list<\stdClass>>
     */
    public function ancestorChains(array $parentIds): array
    {
        if ($parentIds === []) {
            return [];
        }
        /** @var \Staudenmeir\LaravelAdjacencyList\Eloquent\Builder<Node> $query */
        $query = $this->query();
        $connection = app(VaulterDatabase::class)->connection();
        $grammar = $query->getExpressionGrammar();
        $table = $connection->getQueryGrammar()->wrapTable($query->getModel()->getTable());
        $separator = $query->getModel()->getPathSeparator();
        $initialPath = $grammar->compileInitialPath('id', '__acl_path');
        $seed = $this->whereKeyInBounded($query, $parentIds)
            ->select(['id', 'parent_id', 'storage_id', 'inherit_grants'])->selectRaw('id as origin, 0 as depth');
        // Match ancestors()'s ascend semantics, including its database-specific
        // cycle guard. withMaxDepth(+64) does not limit negative ancestor depths.
        $path = $grammar->compileRecursivePath('n.id', 'c.__acl_path');
        $cycle = $grammar->compileCycleDetection('n.id', 'c.__acl_path');
        $sql = 'WITH RECURSIVE chain AS (SELECT seed.*, '.$initialPath.' FROM ('.$seed->toSql().') seed
            UNION ALL SELECT n.id, n.parent_id, n.storage_id, n.inherit_grants, c.origin, c.depth + 1, '.$path.'
            FROM '.$table.' n JOIN chain c ON n.id = c.parent_id AND n.storage_id = c.storage_id
            WHERE c.inherit_grants = ? AND NOT ('.$cycle.') AND n.deleted_at IS NULL)
            SELECT * FROM chain ORDER BY origin, depth';
        $rows = $connection->select($sql, [
            ...$seed->getBindings(),
            ...$grammar->getRecursivePathBindings($separator),
            true,
            ...$grammar->getCycleDetectionBindings($separator),
        ]);
        $chains = [];
        foreach ($rows as $row) {
            if (!$row instanceof \stdClass || (!is_string($row->origin) && !is_int($row->origin))) {
                throw new \LogicException('Invalid ancestor chain row.');
            }
            $chains[Identity::stringify($row->origin)][] = $row;
        }

        return $chains;
    }

    /**
     * The ancestor chain from the node up to the storage root (nearest first),
     * bounded by withMaxDepth (F-P4.2-01, VLT-REMED P4.3) — the same cap
     * descendants()/subtree() already apply on the other traversal direction
     * (ADR-0005 requires the symmetry; this was the missing ascend-side half).
     *
     * @return Collection<int, Node>
     */
    public function ancestors(Node $node): Collection
    {
        $model = Config::nodeModel();

        /** @var Collection<int, Node> $result */
        $result = $model::withMaxDepth(
            self::DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH,
            static fn (): Collection => $node->ancestors()->get(),
        );

        return $result;
    }

    /**
     * Breadcrumb from the storage root down to the node (root first), the node
     * included as the last crumb. Bounded by withMaxDepth (F-P4.2-01) — same
     * reasoning as ancestors() above.
     *
     * @return Collection<int, Node>
     */
    public function breadcrumb(Node $node): Collection
    {
        return $this->metrics->timing(
            name: 'vaulter_tree_query_duration_seconds',
            operation: function () use ($node): Collection {
                $model = Config::nodeModel();

                /** @var Collection<int, Node> $crumbs */
                $crumbs = $model::withMaxDepth(
                    self::DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH,
                    static fn (): Collection => $node->ancestorsAndSelf()->get(),
                );

                /** @var Collection<int, Node> $ordered */
                $ordered = $crumbs->reverse()->values();

                return $ordered;
            },
            labels: [
                'query_kind' => 'breadcrumb',
                'tier' => LoadTier::label(),
            ],
        );
    }

    /**
     * Name search within a storage tree (live nodes only). Matches a case-folded
     * substring of the node name — the §2.2 search index backs this.
     *
     * The caller-supplied term is untrusted (F-P1.8-03): `LIKE` metacharacters
     * (`%`, `_`, `\`) are escaped BEFORE the substring wildcards are wrapped
     * around it, with an explicit `ESCAPE '\'` clause — a raw `%`/`_` in the
     * term is matched LITERALLY, never as a wildcard, on both SQLite and
     * PostgreSQL. Case-folding happens first so escaping never touches
     * multibyte case-mapping. `orderBy('id')` is a stable tie-breaker
     * (F-P4.2-05, VLT-REMED P4.3): `name` is unique only per-parent (ADR-0005)
     * and this method searches across the WHOLE tree, so cursorPaginate()'s
     * keyset would otherwise skip/duplicate rows across a page boundary that
     * lands on two nodes sharing a name in different folders.
     *
     * @return Builder<Node>
     */
    public function search(Storage $storage, string $term): Builder
    {
        return $this->metrics->timing(
            name: 'vaulter_tree_query_duration_seconds',
            operation: function () use ($storage, $term): Builder {
                $escaped = addcslashes(mb_strtolower($term), '%_\\');

                return $this->query()
                    ->treeOf($storage->id)
                    ->whereNull('trashed_at')
                    ->whereRaw("lower(name) like ? escape '\\'", ['%'.$escaped.'%'])
                    ->orderBy('name')
                    ->orderBy('id');
            },
            labels: [
                'query_kind' => 'search',
                'tier' => LoadTier::label(),
            ],
        );
    }

    /**
     * The trash view: trashed (but not yet permanently deleted) nodes in a
     * storage, newest first. `orderByDesc('id')` is a stable tie-breaker
     * (F-P4.2-05, VLT-REMED P4.3): several nodes trashed by the same
     * operation/request share an identical trashed_at timestamp, so
     * cursorPaginate()'s keyset would otherwise skip/duplicate rows on that
     * page boundary.
     *
     * @return Builder<Node>
     */
    public function trashed(Storage $storage): Builder
    {
        return $this->query()
            ->treeOf($storage->id)
            ->whereNotNull('trashed_at')
            ->orderByDesc('trashed_at')
            ->orderByDesc('id');
    }

    /**
     * The trashed nodes whose trashed_at is at or before $threshold — the
     * purge-trash candidates past their retention window (§2.10). Only ROOTS of a
     * trash operation are returned: a node whose own parent is itself trashed is
     * skipped, because force-deleting an ancestor's subtree already reclaims it (so
     * a child is never purged twice). Returns a Builder so the command chunks it.
     *
     * @return Builder<Node>
     */
    public function trashedBefore(Carbon $threshold): Builder
    {
        $model = Config::nodeModel();
        $table = (new $model)->getTable();

        return $this->query()
            ->whereNotNull('trashed_at')
            ->where('trashed_at', '<=', $threshold)
            // Skip descendants whose own parent is also trashed — the trashed
            // ancestor is the purge root and its subtree force-delete covers them.
            ->whereNotExists(static function (QueryBuilder $sub) use ($table): void {
                $sub->selectRaw('1')
                    ->from($table.' as parent_node')
                    ->whereColumn('parent_node.id', $table.'.parent_id')
                    ->whereNotNull('parent_node.trashed_at');
            })
            ->orderBy('trashed_at');
    }

    /**
     * Whether a live sibling already owns $name under $parentId in this storage
     * (case-insensitive) — the §2.2 sibling-unique predicate the Actions check
     * before create/move/rename. $ignoreId excludes the node being renamed in
     * place. Uses a direct EXISTS, NOT a recursive descendant aggregate (H.4).
     */
    public function siblingNameExists(
        Storage $storage,
        int|string|null $parentId,
        string $name,
        int|string|null $ignoreId = null,
    ): bool {
        $query = $this->query()
            ->treeOf($storage->id)
            ->where('parent_id', $parentId)
            ->whereNull('trashed_at')
            ->whereRaw('lower(name) = ?', [mb_strtolower($name)]);

        if ($ignoreId !== null) {
            $query->whereKeyNot($ignoreId);
        }

        return $query->exists();
    }

    /**
     * The STANDALONE public seam (master plan P1 #3): the live (non-trashed) nodes an
     * actor may reach, as a Builder for pagination / Filament. It is the named entry
     * for "give me this actor's nodes" = the live-nodes base + constrainToAccessible.
     * A caller that needs its own trashed-state control (the children cursor below, or
     * the Filament table whose Trashed filter flips trashed_at) composes
     * {@see constrainToAccessible()} on its own base instead.
     *
     * When grant-filtering applies (see {@see grantFilteringApplies()}) the base is
     * constrained to the actor's CANDIDATE accessible node ids; otherwise the base is
     * returned UNCHANGED (open listing — the host's roles govern).
     *
     * The candidate set is the grant ANCHORS for the actor's subject-set UNION the
     * descendants of every SUBTREE-scoped anchor (an item-scoped anchor contributes
     * only its own node). It is a deliberate SUPERSET: it does NOT apply the deny-veto
     * or the inherit_grants cut-off in SQL, so a node a nearer deny revokes can still
     * be in the set. The candidate-set listing semantic is documented on
     * {@see constrainToAccessible()}; per-ACTION authorization stays EXACT via the
     * {@see AccessResolver} on every mutation/show.
     *
     * @return Builder<Node>
     */
    public function queryForUser(Authorizable $actor): Builder
    {
        $base = $this->query()->whereNull('trashed_at');

        return $this->constrainToAccessible($base, $actor);
    }

    /**
     * Constrain an ALREADY-BUILT node query to the actor's grant-reachable candidate
     * set (P1 #3), WITHOUT imposing the live/trashed filter — so a caller that owns
     * its own trashed-state handling (the children cursor; the Filament table, whose
